Well, I guess what brings me in is... I keep making myself throw up after I eat. It's getting really bad. Well, it's... it's a lot. For months now, I'd say most weeks have at least a couple of times where I just lose control and eat way more than I ever meant to. And then I have to fix it, you know? So I throw up, or I just don't eat at all the next day, or I try to walk it off for hours. I actually walk down by the old canal when I do that. It's supposed to be this nice walking path now, the city spent all this money putting in these benches and those little solar lights, but half the lights are already broken. Kids come down there and smash them, I think. I saw a group of teenagers down there last Tuesday just hanging out, smoking, and I thought about saying something, but you never know these days, right? You say the wrong thing and suddenly you're the bad guy. But yeah, the eating and then trying to undo it... it's at least a couple times a week, sometimes more.

Yeah, I mean... it's the main reason, definitely. I just can't stand the thought of getting bigger. It's like this voice in my head that just won't shut up about it. No, not at all. Once I start eating, it feels like my hands just keep reaching for food even while I'm telling myself to stop. It's like I'm watching myself do it from the outside and I just cannot make myself quit. It's the weirdest feeling, almost like being on autopilot. Speaking of autopilot, my car has been doing this strange thing lately where the cruise control won't turn off right away when I tap the brakes. It's a 2008 Honda, so it's getting up there in miles. I took it to my mechanic, Luis—he's got this shop down by the old textile mill—and he said it might be a sensor issue. Luis is great, though. He always has these little hard candies on his counter, the strawberry ones with the gooey centers? I love those. He said his wife buys them in bulk from some warehouse club. I should probably ask him which one, because they're really good. But yeah, with the food... I just can't stop once I start. There's no control at all.

I tried talking to them about it, they live in 4B, the couple with the weird green car... it's like a neon green, you can't miss it in the parking lot. Anyway, I asked if they could maybe keep the dog inside during the day when they're at work, but they said he has separation anxiety. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, sometimes... I mean, if I don't throw up, I still have to do something about it, right? So on the days after I eat way too much and don't get sick, I try to make up for it by just not eating anything at all until really late at night. Or I'll force myself to walk for miles and exercise, even when my legs are absolutely throbbing from standing at the cleaners all day. 

Working the steam press is just brutal on your feet. I actually bought these memory foam inserts for my sneakers recently, the ones they advertise on TV? The commercial has this guy dropping a bowling ball on the foam, which makes zero sense when you actually think about it. Like, who is dropping bowling balls on their shoes? But I bought them anyway at the pharmacy next to the dry cleaners. The pharmacist there, Brenda, she always wears these wild earrings. Yesterday she had on these little miniature flamingos. I asked her where she gets them, and she said she makes them herself out of polymer clay in her oven. I tried getting into crafts once, knitting mostly, but I just ended up with a bunch of tangled yarn in a garbage bag. 

But yeah... if I don't get rid of the food that way, I just starve myself the next day or walk until I can barely feel my feet. I just have to make up for it somehow.

Sometimes I even do it in the bathroom at work, which is so risky because the machines are loud, but if someone walks in, you know? I work at this big commercial laundry place, and the steam presses are always hissing, so that helps cover the noise, but still. Or I'll do it right when I get home. My neighbor's dog is always barking when I get home, this little terrier mix that just yaps at every single car that drives by. It drives me crazy, honestly. But yeah, the throwing up... I hate it, I really do, but it’s started feeling like the only way I can calm down.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... it's a lot. For months now, I'd say most weeks have at least a couple of times where I just lose control and eat way more than I ever meant to. And then I have to fix it, you know? So I throw up, or I just don't eat at all the next day, or I try to walk it off for hours. I actually walk down by the old canal when I do that. It's supposed to be this nice walking path now, the city spent all this money putting in these benches and those little solar lights, but half the lights are already broken. Kids come down there and smash them, I think. I saw a group of teenagers down there last Tuesday just hanging out, smoking, and I thought about saying something, but you never know these days, right? You say the wrong thing and suddenly you're the bad guy. But yeah, the eating and then trying to undo it... it's at least a couple times a week, sometimes more.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, god, yes. I'm terrified of it. It's like... if the scale goes up even a tiny bit, or if my waistband feels even a little tight under my work apron, I just panic. I feel like I've ruined everything and I'm going to be completely fat by the end of the day. Speaking of my apron, they just switched our uniform supplier at work, and the new ones are this awful stiff material. They don't breathe at all, which is terrible when you're working around those giant industrial dryers all day. The old ones were much softer, kind of a cotton blend, I think. I asked my manager, Dave, if we could go back to the old ones, but he said corporate signed a two-year contract, so we're stuck with them. Dave's nice enough, but he never really stands up to corporate, you know? He just kind of nods and does whatever they say. But yeah, the fear of gaining weight... it's always there. It's exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without throwing up afterwards?\n\nPatient: Well, no... I mean, I always end up having to fix it afterwards. But when I do start eating, it's like I can't stop. I'll go through a whole family-size bag of chips, and then maybe some pastries I picked up from the corner store on my way home. The guy who runs that store, Mr. Patel, he's always so friendly, always asks about my day. He's got this old cash register that makes this loud *ding* sound every time he opens it, it's almost vintage, you know? I think he said his grandfather bought it back in the seventies or something. But yeah, I'll eat the pastries, and then whatever leftovers are in the fridge, and wash it all down with a bunch of sweet drinks... all in one sitting. It's like I don't even realize how much I've eaten until it's all gone and the wrappers are just sitting there. And then the panic sets in, and I have to go throw it up.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the main reason you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... it's the main reason, definitely. I just can't stand the thought of getting bigger. It's like this voice in my head that just won't shut up about it. Just like my neighbor's dog, actually. They got this little terrier mix a few weeks ago, and it barks at absolutely everything. A leaf blows across the sidewalk? Barking. The mail carrier comes? Nonstop barking. I tried talking to them about it, they live in 4B, the couple with the weird green car... it's like a neon green, you can't miss it in the parking lot. Anyway, I asked if they could maybe keep the dog inside during the day when they're at work, but they said he has separation anxiety. Which, I get it, animals have feelings too, but it's driving me crazy when I'm trying to sleep after a long shift. But yeah, the fear of gaining weight... that's why I do it. I just have to get it out before it turns into fat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating these large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Once I start eating, it feels like my hands just keep reaching for food even while I'm telling myself to stop. It's like I'm watching myself do it from the outside and I just cannot make myself quit. It's the weirdest feeling, almost like being on autopilot. Speaking of autopilot, my car has been doing this strange thing lately where the cruise control won't turn off right away when I tap the brakes. It's a 2008 Honda, so it's getting up there in miles. I took it to my mechanic, Luis—he's got this shop down by the old textile mill—and he said it might be a sensor issue. Luis is great, though. He always has these little hard candies on his counter, the strawberry ones with the gooey centers? I love those. He said his wife buys them in bulk from some warehouse club. I should probably ask him which one, because they're really good. But yeah, with the food... I just can't stop once I start. There's no control at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time and then not thrown up afterwards?\n\nPatient: Well, sometimes... I mean, if I don't throw up, I still have to do something about it, right? So on the days after I eat way too much and don't get sick, I try to make up for it by just not eating anything at all until really late at night. Or I'll force myself to walk for miles and exercise, even when my legs are absolutely throbbing from standing at the cleaners all day. \n\nWorking the steam press is just brutal on your feet. I actually bought these memory foam inserts for my sneakers recently, the ones they advertise on TV? The commercial has this guy dropping a bowling ball on the foam, which makes zero sense when you actually think about it. Like, who is dropping bowling balls on their shoes? But I bought them anyway at the pharmacy next to the dry cleaners. The pharmacist there, Brenda, she always wears these wild earrings. Yesterday she had on these little miniature flamingos. I asked her where she gets them, and she said she makes them herself out of polymer clay in her oven. I tried getting into crafts once, knitting mostly, but I just ended up with a bunch of tangled yarn in a garbage bag. \n\nBut yeah... if I don't get rid of the food that way, I just starve myself the next day or walk until I can barely feel my feet. I just have to make up for it somehow.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

{"candidates": ["F50.2", "F50.0"], "note": "", "is_final": false}

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I don't think so. I mean, I always have to do *something*. If I didn't, I'd just sit there feeling it all turning into fat, and I can't handle that. It's like this heavy, awful feeling. Actually, speaking of heavy, you wouldn't believe the bags of laundry some people bring in. Just yesterday, this guy brought in a duffel bag—one of those massive military-style ones—completely stuffed with wool sweaters. Who wears that many wool sweaters in this weather? And they smell like mothballs, which is just the worst smell. It gets right up into your sinuses and stays there all day. I tried drinking peppermint tea to clear it out, because my aunt used to say peppermint cures everything. She used to grow it in her backyard in these old coffee cans. Folgers, I think. The red plastic ones. She'd paint them with these little daisies. She was so artistic like that, not like me with my tangled knitting yarn. But no, to answer your question, I never just let it sit there. I always have to get rid of it or work it off somehow. I just have to.

I work at this big commercial laundry place, and the steam presses are always hissing, so that helps cover the noise, but still. Or I'll do it right when I get home. My neighbor's dog is always barking when I get home, this little terrier mix that just yaps at every single car that drives by. It drives me crazy, honestly. But yeah, the throwing up... I hate it, I really do, but it’s started feeling like the only way I can calm down.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... it's a lot. For months now, I'd say most weeks have at least a couple of times where I just lose control and eat way more than I ever meant to. And then I have to fix it, you know? So I throw up, or I just don't eat at all the next day, or I try to walk it off for hours. I actually walk down by the old canal when I do that. It's supposed to be this nice walking path now, the city spent all this money putting in these benches and those little solar lights, but half the lights are already broken. Kids come down there and smash them, I think. I saw a group of teenagers down there last Tuesday just hanging out, smoking, and I thought about saying something, but you never know these days, right? You say the wrong thing and suddenly you're the bad guy. But yeah, the eating and then trying to undo it... it's at least a couple times a week, sometimes more.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, god, yes. I'm terrified of it. It's like... if the scale goes up even a tiny bit, or if my waistband feels even a little tight under my work apron, I just panic. I feel like I've ruined everything and I'm going to be completely fat by the end of the day. Speaking of my apron, they just switched our uniform supplier at work, and the new ones are this awful stiff material. They don't breathe at all, which is terrible when you're working around those giant industrial dryers all day. The old ones were much softer, kind of a cotton blend, I think. I asked my manager, Dave, if we could go back to the old ones, but he said corporate signed a two-year contract, so we're stuck with them. Dave's nice enough, but he never really stands up to corporate, you know? He just kind of nods and does whatever they say. But yeah, the fear of gaining weight... it's always there. It's exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without throwing up afterwards?\n\nPatient: Well, no... I mean, I always end up having to fix it afterwards. But when I do start eating, it's like I can't stop. I'll go through a whole family-size bag of chips, and then maybe some pastries I picked up from the corner store on my way home. The guy who runs that store, Mr. Patel, he's always so friendly, always asks about my day. He's got this old cash register that makes this loud *ding* sound every time he opens it, it's almost vintage, you know? I think he said his grandfather bought it back in the seventies or something. But yeah, I'll eat the pastries, and then whatever leftovers are in the fridge, and wash it all down with a bunch of sweet drinks... all in one sitting. It's like I don't even realize how much I've eaten until it's all gone and the wrappers are just sitting there. And then the panic sets in, and I have to go throw it up.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the main reason you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... it's the main reason, definitely. I just can't stand the thought of getting bigger. It's like this voice in my head that just won't shut up about it. Just like my neighbor's dog, actually. They got this little terrier mix a few weeks ago, and it barks at absolutely everything. A leaf blows across the sidewalk? Barking. The mail carrier comes? Nonstop barking. I tried talking to them about it, they live in 4B, the couple with the weird green car... it's like a neon green, you can't miss it in the parking lot. Anyway, I asked if they could maybe keep the dog inside during the day when they're at work, but they said he has separation anxiety. Which, I get it, animals have feelings too, but it's driving me crazy when I'm trying to sleep after a long shift. But yeah, the fear of gaining weight... that's why I do it. I just have to get it out before it turns into fat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating these large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Once I start eating, it feels like my hands just keep reaching for food even while I'm telling myself to stop. It's like I'm watching myself do it from the outside and I just cannot make myself quit. It's the weirdest feeling, almost like being on autopilot. Speaking of autopilot, my car has been doing this strange thing lately where the cruise control won't turn off right away when I tap the brakes. It's a 2008 Honda, so it's getting up there in miles. I took it to my mechanic, Luis—he's got this shop down by the old textile mill—and he said it might be a sensor issue. Luis is great, though. He always has these little hard candies on his counter, the strawberry ones with the gooey centers? I love those. He said his wife buys them in bulk from some warehouse club. I should probably ask him which one, because they're really good. But yeah, with the food... I just can't stop once I start. There's no control at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time and then not thrown up afterwards?\n\nPatient: Well, sometimes... I mean, if I don't throw up, I still have to do something about it, right? So on the days after I eat way too much and don't get sick, I try to make up for it by just not eating anything at all until really late at night. Or I'll force myself to walk for miles and exercise, even when my legs are absolutely throbbing from standing at the cleaners all day. \n\nWorking the steam press is just brutal on your feet. I actually bought these memory foam inserts for my sneakers recently, the ones they advertise on TV? The commercial has this guy dropping a bowling ball on the foam, which makes zero sense when you actually think about it. Like, who is dropping bowling balls on their shoes? But I bought them anyway at the pharmacy next to the dry cleaners. The pharmacist there, Brenda, she always wears these wild earrings. Yesterday she had on these little miniature flamingos. I asked her where she gets them, and she said she makes them herself out of polymer clay in her oven. I tried getting into crafts once, knitting mostly, but I just ended up with a bunch of tangled yarn in a garbage bag. \n\nBut yeah... if I don't get rid of the food that way, I just starve myself the next day or walk until I can barely feel my feet. I just have to make up for it somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a very large amount of food in a short time and then not done anything to try to get rid of the calories?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. There's no control at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time and then not thrown up afterwards?\n\nPatient: Well, sometimes... I mean, if I don't throw up, I still have to do something about it, right? So on the days after I eat way too much and don't get sick, I try to make up for it by just not eating anything at all until really late at night. Or I'll force myself to walk for miles and exercise, even when my legs are absolutely throbbing from standing at the cleaners all day. \n\nWorking the steam press is just brutal on your feet. I actually bought these memory foam inserts for my sneakers recently, the ones they advertise on TV? The commercial has this guy dropping a bowling ball on the foam, which makes zero sense when you actually think about it. Like, who is dropping bowling balls on their shoes? But I bought them anyway at the pharmacy next to the dry cleaners. The pharmacist there, Brenda, she always wears these wild earrings. Yesterday she had on these little miniature flamingos. I asked her where she gets them, and she said she makes them herself out of polymer clay in her oven. I tried getting into crafts once, knitting mostly, but I just ended up with a bunch of tangled yarn in a garbage bag. \n\nBut yeah... if I don't get rid of the food that way, I just starve myself the next day or walk until I can barely feel my feet. I just have to make up for it somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a very large amount of food in a short time and then not done anything to try to get rid of the calories?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I always have to do *something*. If I didn't, I'd just sit there feeling it all turning into fat, and I can't handle that. It's like this heavy, awful feeling. Actually, speaking of heavy, you wouldn't believe the bags of laundry some people bring in. Just yesterday, this guy brought in a duffel bag—one of those massive military-style ones—completely stuffed with wool sweaters. Who wears that many wool sweaters in this weather? And they smell like mothballs, which is just the worst smell. It gets right up into your sinuses and stays there all day. I tried drinking peppermint tea to clear it out, because my aunt used to say peppermint cures everything. She used to grow it in her backyard in these old coffee cans. Folgers, I think. The red plastic ones. She'd paint them with these little daisies. She was so artistic like that, not like me with my tangled knitting yarn. But no, to answer your question, I never just let it sit there. I always have to get rid of it or work it off somehow. I just have to.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without doing anything to try to get rid of the calories afterwards?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 36 / laundry_or_dry_cleaning_worker / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I just... didn't I just say that? No, I never do that. I always have to do something to make up for it, or I'd just go crazy thinking about it. Like I said, I have to walk or starve or... whatever. I always do something. It's like a rule in my head. Speaking of rules, the owner at the cleaners just put up this new sign about breaks. We used to be able to just take our fifteen minutes whenever things were slow, but now he wants us to clock out exactly at 10:15 and 2:15. It's so rigid. And the time clock is this ancient punch-card thing that jams half the time. My coworker, Rosa—she's the one who does the alterations—she actually got her cardigan sleeve caught in it last week. It was a nice cardigan, too, this really pretty mustard yellow. She said she got it on clearance at that department store that's closing down in the mall. I haven't been to the mall in ages. The parking is just a nightmare, especially near the food court entrance. But yeah, no, I never just eat all that and do nothing. I always have to fix it.

undo it by throwing up, fasting, or exercising it off.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: My days get arranged around hiding what happened, and I have skipped after-work meals and rushed through errands or work tasks because I was preoccupied with how I was going to make up for eating.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 6 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about six months, since I started trying to remake my habits and image for the sustainable-textile showcase.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 36 / laundry_or_dry_cleaning_worker / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S048\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time and then not thrown up afterwards?\n\nPatient: Well, sometimes... I mean, if I don't throw up, I still have to do something about it, right? So on the days after I eat way too much and don't get sick, I try to make up for it by just not eating anything at all until really late at night. Or I'll force myself to walk for miles and exercise, even when my legs are absolutely throbbing from standing at the cleaners all day. \n\nWorking the steam press is just brutal on your feet. I actually bought these memory foam inserts for my sneakers recently, the ones they advertise on TV? The commercial has this guy dropping a bowling ball on the foam, which makes zero sense when you actually think about it. Like, who is dropping bowling balls on their shoes? I asked her where she gets them, and she said she makes them herself out of polymer clay in her oven. I tried getting into crafts once, knitting mostly, but I just ended up with a bunch of tangled yarn in a garbage bag. \n\nBut yeah... if I don't get rid of the food that way, I just starve myself the next day or walk until I can barely feel my feet. I just have to make up for it somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a very large amount of food in a short time and then not done anything to try to get rid of the calories?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I always have to do *something*. If I didn't, I'd just sit there feeling it all turning into fat, and I can't handle that. It's like this heavy, awful feeling. Actually, speaking of heavy, you wouldn't believe the bags of laundry some people bring in. Just yesterday, this guy brought in a duffel bag—one of those massive military-style ones—completely stuffed with wool sweaters. Who wears that many wool sweaters in this weather? And they smell like mothballs, which is just the worst smell. It gets right up into your sinuses and stays there all day. I tried drinking peppermint tea to clear it out, because my aunt used to say peppermint cures everything. She used to grow it in her backyard in these old coffee cans. Folgers, I think. The red plastic ones. She'd paint them with these little daisies. She was so artistic like that, not like me with my tangled knitting yarn. But no, to answer your question, I never just let it sit there. I always have to get rid of it or work it off somehow. I just have to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without doing anything to try to get rid of the calories afterwards?\n\nPatient: I just... didn't I just say that? No, I never do that. I always have to do something to make up for it, or I'd just go crazy thinking about it. Like I said, I have to walk or starve or... whatever. I always do something. It's like a rule in my head. Speaking of rules, the owner at the cleaners just put up this new sign about breaks. We used to be able to just take our fifteen minutes whenever things were slow, but now he wants us to clock out exactly at 10:15 and 2:15. It's so rigid. It was a nice cardigan, too, this really pretty mustard yellow. She said she got it on clearance at that department store that's closing down in the mall. I haven't been to the mall in ages. The parking is just a nightmare, especially near the food court entrance. But yeah, no, I never just eat all that and do nothing. I always have to fix it.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had an episode where you ate a large amount of food in a short time without doing anything afterwards to try to get rid of the calories?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Are you... are you serious right now? I feel like I'm taking crazy pills. I just told you, like, three times. No. Never. I *always* have to do something. I can't just let it sit there. 

It's like you're not even listening to me. My ex-boyfriend used to do that. Gary. You'd be talking right to his face and his eyes would just glaze over like he was watching a TV show behind my forehead. He was a mechanic, always had grease under his fingernails no matter how much he scrubbed. He used this orange pumice soap that smelled like fake citrus. I hate that smell. It reminds me of the bathroom cleaner they use at the bus station. I had to take the bus for a month when my car broke down—the alternator went out. It was a nightmare. The bus driver was this guy who always whistled show tunes. *Phantom of the Opera*, mostly. 

But seriously, doc, how many times are you going to ask me the exact same thing? The answer is no, I never just do nothing. I always fix it. Always.

Which, I get it, animals have feelings too, but it's driving me crazy when I'm trying to sleep after a long shift. But yeah, the fear of gaining weight... that's why I do it. I just have to get it out before it turns into fat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating these large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Once I start eating, it feels like my hands just keep reaching for food even while I'm telling myself to stop. It's like I'm watching myself do it from the outside and I just cannot make myself quit. It's the weirdest feeling, almost like being on autopilot. Speaking of autopilot, my car has been doing this strange thing lately where the cruise control won't turn off right away when I tap the brakes. It's a 2008 Honda, so it's getting up there in miles. I took it to my mechanic, Luis—he's got this shop down by the old textile mill—and he said it might be a sensor issue. Luis is great, though. He always has these little hard candies on his counter, the strawberry ones with the gooey centers? I love those. He said his wife buys them in bulk from some warehouse club. I should probably ask him which one, because they're really good. But yeah, with the food... I just can't stop once I start. There's no control at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time and then not thrown up afterwards?\n\nPatient: Well, sometimes... I mean, if I don't throw up, I still have to do something about it, right? So on the days after I eat way too much and don't get sick, I try to make up for it by just not eating anything at all until really late at night. Or I'll force myself to walk for miles and exercise, even when my legs are absolutely throbbing from standing at the cleaners all day. \n\nWorking the steam press is just brutal on your feet. I actually bought these memory foam inserts for my sneakers recently, the ones they advertise on TV? The commercial has this guy dropping a bowling ball on the foam, which makes zero sense when you actually think about it. Like, who is dropping bowling balls on their shoes? But I bought them anyway at the pharmacy next to the dry cleaners. The pharmacist there, Brenda, she always wears these wild earrings. Yesterday she had on these little miniature flamingos. I asked her where she gets them, and she said she makes them herself out of polymer clay in her oven. I tried getting into crafts once, knitting mostly, but I just ended up with a bunch of tangled yarn in a garbage bag. \n\nBut yeah... if I don't get rid of the food that way, I just starve myself the next day or walk until I can barely feel my feet. I just have to make up for it somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a very large amount of food in a short time and then not done anything to try to get rid of the calories?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I always have to do *something*. If I didn't, I'd just sit there feeling it all turning into fat, and I can't handle that. It's like this heavy, awful feeling. Actually, speaking of heavy, you wouldn't believe the bags of laundry some people bring in. Just yesterday, this guy brought in a duffel bag—one of those massive military-style ones—completely stuffed with wool sweaters. Who wears that many wool sweaters in this weather? And they smell like mothballs, which is just the worst smell. It gets right up into your sinuses and stays there all day. I tried drinking peppermint tea to clear it out, because my aunt used to say peppermint cures everything. She used to grow it in her backyard in these old coffee cans. Folgers, I think. The red plastic ones. She'd paint them with these little daisies. She was so artistic like that, not like me with my tangled knitting yarn. I just have to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without doing anything to try to get rid of the calories afterwards?\n\nPatient: I just... didn't I just say that? It's like a rule in my head. Speaking of rules, the owner at the cleaners just put up this new sign about breaks. We used to be able to just take our fifteen minutes whenever things were slow, but now he wants us to clock out exactly at 10:15 and 2:15. It's so rigid. It was a nice cardigan, too, this really pretty mustard yellow. She said she got it on clearance at that department store that's closing down in the mall. I haven't been to the mall in ages. I just... I honestly don't know how else to say it to you. No. There is no instance. Not one. I never, ever just eat all that and do nothing. 

You're like a broken record, you know that? It actually reminds me of the CD player we have at the front counter at the cleaners. The laser must be dirty or scratched or something because it always skips on track four. It's this old Michael Bublé Christmas album that my manager refuses to take out of the machine, even though it's not even close to the holidays. I tried to clean it last week with one of those special discs, you know the ones with the tiny little brushes glued to the bottom? But I think I just made it worse. Rosa told me I should just accidentally knock the whole stereo into the trash can. Honestly, I'm tempted. Though our dumpsters out back smell so bad right now I don't even want to go near them. The seafood place next door keeps leaking their old frying oil into the alley, and the stray cats come and lick it up. It's so gross. 

But yeah, to answer your question again, no. I never do that. I always have to get rid of it.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess what brings me in is... I keep making myself throw up after I eat. It's getting really bad. I work at this big commercial laundry place, and the steam presses are always hissing, so that helps cover the noise, but still. Or I'll do it right when I get home. My neighbor's dog is always barking when I get home, this little terrier mix that just yaps at every single car that drives by. It drives me crazy, honestly. But yeah, the throwing up... I hate it, I really do, but it’s started feeling like the only way I can calm down.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... it's a lot. For months now, I'd say most weeks have at least a couple of times where I just lose control and eat way more than I ever meant to. And then I have to fix it, you know? So I throw up, or I just don't eat at all the next day, or I try to walk it off for hours. I actually walk down by the old canal when I do that. It's supposed to be this nice walking path now, the city spent all this money putting in these benches and those little solar lights, but half the lights are already broken. Kids come down there and smash them, I think. I saw a group of teenagers down there last Tuesday just hanging out, smoking, and I thought about saying something, but you never know these days, right? You say the wrong thing and suddenly you're the bad guy. But yeah, the eating and then trying to undo it... it's at least a couple times a week, sometimes more.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, god, yes. I'm terrified of it. It's like... if the scale goes up even a tiny bit, or if my waistband feels even a little tight under my work apron, I just panic. I feel like I've ruined everything and I'm going to be completely fat by the end of the day. Speaking of my apron, they just switched our uniform supplier at work, and the new ones are this awful stiff material. They don't breathe at all, which is terrible when you're working around those giant industrial dryers all day. The old ones were much softer, kind of a cotton blend, I think. I asked my manager, Dave, if we could go back to the old ones, but he said corporate signed a two-year contract, so we're stuck with them. Dave's nice enough, but he never really stands up to corporate, you know? He just kind of nods and does whatever they say. But yeah, the fear of gaining weight... it's always there. It's exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without throwing up afterwards?\n\nPatient: Well, no... I mean, I always end up having to fix it afterwards. But when I do start eating, it's like I can't stop. I'll go through a whole family-size bag of chips, and then maybe some pastries I picked up from the corner store on my way home. The guy who runs that store, Mr. Patel, he's always so friendly, always asks about my day. He's got this old cash register that makes this loud *ding* sound every time he opens it, it's almost vintage, you know? I think he said his grandfather bought it back in the seventies or something. But yeah, I'll eat the pastries, and then whatever leftovers are in the fridge, and wash it all down with a bunch of sweet drinks... all in one sitting. It's like I don't even realize how much I've eaten until it's all gone and the wrappers are just sitting there. And then the panic sets in, and I have to go throw it up.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the main reason you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... it's the main reason, definitely. I just can't stand the thought of getting bigger. It's like this voice in my head that just won't shut up about it. Just like my neighbor's dog, actually. They got this little terrier mix a few weeks ago, and it barks at absolutely everything. A leaf blows across the sidewalk? Barking. The mail carrier comes? Nonstop barking. I tried talking to them about it, they live in 4B, the couple with the weird green car... it's like a neon green, you can't miss it in the parking lot. Anyway, I asked if they could maybe keep the dog inside during the day when they're at work, but they said he has separation anxiety. Which, I get it, animals have feelings too, but it's driving me crazy when I'm trying to sleep after a long shift. But yeah, the fear of gaining weight... that's why I do it. I just have to get it out before it turns into fat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating these large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Once I start eating, it feels like my hands just keep reaching for food even while I'm telling myself to stop. It's like I'm watching myself do it from the outside and I just cannot make myself quit. It's the weirdest feeling, almost like being on autopilot. Speaking of autopilot, my car has been doing this strange thing lately where the cruise control won't turn off right away when I tap the brakes. It's a 2008 Honda, so it's getting up there in miles. I took it to my mechanic, Luis—he's got this shop down by the old textile mill—and he said it might be a sensor issue. Luis is great, though. He always has these little hard candies on his counter, the strawberry ones with the gooey centers? I love those. He said his wife buys them in bulk from some warehouse club. I should probably ask him which one, because they're really good. But yeah, with the food... I just can't stop once I start. There's no control at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time and then not thrown up afterwards?\n\nPatient: Well, sometimes... I mean, if I don't throw up, I still have to do something about it, right? So on the days after I eat way too much and don't get sick, I try to make up for it by just not eating anything at all until really late at night. Or I'll force myself to walk for miles and exercise, even when my legs are absolutely throbbing from standing at the cleaners all day. \n\nWorking the steam press is just brutal on your feet. I actually bought these memory foam inserts for my sneakers recently, the ones they advertise on TV? The commercial has this guy dropping a bowling ball on the foam, which makes zero sense when you actually think about it. Like, who is dropping bowling balls on their shoes? My ex-boyfriend used to do that. Gary. You'd be talking right to his face and his eyes would just glaze over like he was watching a TV show behind my forehead. He was a mechanic, always had grease under his fingernails no matter how much he scrubbed. He used this orange pumice soap that smelled like fake citrus. I hate that smell. It reminds me of the bathroom cleaner they use at the bus station. I had to take the bus for a month when my car broke down—the alternator went out. It was a nightmare. The bus driver was this guy who always whistled show tunes. *Phantom of the Opera*, mostly. \n\nBut seriously, doc, how many times are you going to ask me the exact same thing? The answer is no, I never just do nothing. I always fix it. Always.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any instance where you ate a large amount of food in a short time and then didn't throw up, exercise, or restrict your food intake afterwards?\n\nPatient: I just... I honestly don't know how else to say it to you. No. There is no instance. Not one. I never, ever just eat all that and do nothing. \n\nYou're like a broken record, you know that? It actually reminds me of the CD player we have at the front counter at the cleaners. The laser must be dirty or scratched or something because it always skips on track four. It's this old Michael Bublé Christmas album that my manager refuses to take out of the machine, even though it's not even close to the holidays. It just skips and repeats the exact same line over and over. It drives me absolutely up the wall. I tried to clean it last week with one of those special discs, you know the ones with the tiny little brushes glued to the bottom? But I think I just made it worse. Rosa told me I should just accidentally knock the whole stereo into the trash can. Honestly, I'm tempted. Though our dumpsters out back smell so bad right now I don't even want to go near them. The seafood place next door keeps leaking their old frying oil into the alley, and the stray cats come and lick it up. It's so gross. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question again, no. I never do that. I always have to get rid of it.\n\n[Last Question]\nIs it the fear of gaining weight that triggers your loss of control when eating?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 36 / laundry_or_dry_cleaning_worker / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don't... I don't really know if it's the fear of gaining weight that starts it. I mean, I am terrified of gaining weight, obviously, but when I lose control, it's just... I'm not sure what triggers it exactly. It just happens. 

Speaking of triggers, the bell above the door at the cleaners is driving me insane lately. Every time someone walks in, it does this really high-pitched jingle. It didn't used to bother me, but the spring is loose or something, so now it rattles for like ten seconds after the door closes. It sets my teeth on edge. Rosa says it sounds like a dog with a rusty collar scratching itself. She actually has two dogs, pugs, I think? Or maybe French bulldogs. One of them has this breathing problem where it snores even when it's awake. She showed me a video on her phone, and the screen was completely cracked. She said she dropped it in the parking lot at the grocery store. The one on Elm Street, you know? They just remodeled the produce section, but they moved all the apples to the other side, and it's so confusing. I spent twenty minutes looking for Granny Smiths last time I was there. 

But yeah, I don't really know what starts the eating. I just know I can't stop once I do.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.2, F50.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess what brings me in is... I keep making myself throw up after I eat. It's getting really bad. Sometimes I even do it in the bathroom at work, which is so risky because the machines are loud, but if someone walks in, you know? I work at this big commercial laundry place, and the steam presses are always hissing, so that helps cover the noise, but still. Or I'll do it right when I get home. My neighbor's dog is always barking when I get home, this little terrier mix that just yaps at every single car that drives by. It drives me crazy, honestly. But yeah, the throwing up... I hate it, I really do, but it’s started feeling like the only way I can calm down.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... it's a lot. For months now, I'd say most weeks have at least a couple of times where I just lose control and eat way more than I ever meant to. And then I have to fix it, you know? So I throw up, or I just don't eat at all the next day, or I try to walk it off for hours. I actually walk down by the old canal when I do that. It's supposed to be this nice walking path now, the city spent all this money putting in these benches and those little solar lights, but half the lights are already broken. Kids come down there and smash them, I think. I saw a group of teenagers down there last Tuesday just hanging out, smoking, and I thought about saying something, but you never know these days, right? You say the wrong thing and suddenly you're the bad guy. But yeah, the eating and then trying to undo it... it's at least a couple times a week, sometimes more.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, god, yes. I'm terrified of it. It's like... if the scale goes up even a tiny bit, or if my waistband feels even a little tight under my work apron, I just panic. I feel like I've ruined everything and I'm going to be completely fat by the end of the day. Speaking of my apron, they just switched our uniform supplier at work, and the new ones are this awful stiff material. They don't breathe at all, which is terrible when you're working around those giant industrial dryers all day. The old ones were much softer, kind of a cotton blend, I think. I asked my manager, Dave, if we could go back to the old ones, but he said corporate signed a two-year contract, so we're stuck with them. Dave's nice enough, but he never really stands up to corporate, you know? He just kind of nods and does whatever they say. But yeah, the fear of gaining weight... it's always there. It's exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without throwing up afterwards?\n\nPatient: Well, no... I mean, I always end up having to fix it afterwards. But when I do start eating, it's like I can't stop. I'll go through a whole family-size bag of chips, and then maybe some pastries I picked up from the corner store on my way home. The guy who runs that store, Mr. Patel, he's always so friendly, always asks about my day. He's got this old cash register that makes this loud *ding* sound every time he opens it, it's almost vintage, you know? I think he said his grandfather bought it back in the seventies or something. But yeah, I'll eat the pastries, and then whatever leftovers are in the fridge, and wash it all down with a bunch of sweet drinks... all in one sitting. It's like I don't even realize how much I've eaten until it's all gone and the wrappers are just sitting there. And then the panic sets in, and I have to go throw it up.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the main reason you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... it's the main reason, definitely. I just can't stand the thought of getting bigger. It's like this voice in my head that just won't shut up about it. Just like my neighbor's dog, actually. They got this little terrier mix a few weeks ago, and it barks at absolutely everything. A leaf blows across the sidewalk? Barking. The mail carrier comes? Nonstop barking. I tried talking to them about it, they live in 4B, the couple with the weird green car... it's like a neon green, you can't miss it in the parking lot. Anyway, I asked if they could maybe keep the dog inside during the day when they're at work, but they said he has separation anxiety. Which, I get it, animals have feelings too, but it's driving me crazy when I'm trying to sleep after a long shift. But yeah, the fear of gaining weight... that's why I do it. I just have to get it out before it turns into fat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating these large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Once I start eating, it feels like my hands just keep reaching for food even while I'm telling myself to stop. It's like I'm watching myself do it from the outside and I just cannot make myself quit. It's the weirdest feeling, almost like being on autopilot. Speaking of autopilot, my car has been doing this strange thing lately where the cruise control won't turn off right away when I tap the brakes. It's a 2008 Honda, so it's getting up there in miles. I took it to my mechanic, Luis—he's got this shop down by the old textile mill—and he said it might be a sensor issue. Luis is great, though. He always has these little hard candies on his counter, the strawberry ones with the gooey centers? I love those. He said his wife buys them in bulk from some warehouse club. I should probably ask him which one, because they're really good. But yeah, with the food... I just can't stop once I start. There's no control at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time and then not thrown up afterwards?\n\nPatient: Well, sometimes... I mean, if I don't throw up, I still have to do something about it, right? So on the days after I eat way too much and don't get sick, I try to make up for it by just not eating anything at all until really late at night. Or I'll force myself to walk for miles and exercise, even when my legs are absolutely throbbing from standing at the cleaners all day. \n\nWorking the steam press is just brutal on your feet. I actually bought these memory foam inserts for my sneakers recently, the ones they advertise on TV? The commercial has this guy dropping a bowling ball on the foam, which makes zero sense when you actually think about it. Like, who is dropping bowling balls on their shoes? But I bought them anyway at the pharmacy next to the dry cleaners. The pharmacist there, Brenda, she always wears these wild earrings. Yesterday she had on these little miniature flamingos. I asked her where she gets them, and she said she makes them herself out of polymer clay in her oven. I tried getting into crafts once, knitting mostly, but I just ended up with a bunch of tangled yarn in a garbage bag. \n\nBut yeah... if I don't get rid of the food that way, I just starve myself the next day or walk until I can barely feel my feet. I just have to make up for it somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a very large amount of food in a short time and then not done anything to try to get rid of the calories?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I always have to do *something*. If I didn't, I'd just sit there feeling it all turning into fat, and I can't handle that. It's like this heavy, awful feeling. Actually, speaking of heavy, you wouldn't believe the bags of laundry some people bring in. Just yesterday, this guy brought in a duffel bag—one of those massive military-style ones—completely stuffed with wool sweaters. Who wears that many wool sweaters in this weather? And they smell like mothballs, which is just the worst smell. It gets right up into your sinuses and stays there all day. I tried drinking peppermint tea to clear it out, because my aunt used to say peppermint cures everything. She used to grow it in her backyard in these old coffee cans. Folgers, I think. The red plastic ones. She'd paint them with these little daisies. She was so artistic like that, not like me with my tangled knitting yarn. But no, to answer your question, I never just let it sit there. I always have to get rid of it or work it off somehow. I just have to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without doing anything to try to get rid of the calories afterwards?\n\nPatient: I just... didn't I just say that? No, I never do that. I always have to do something to make up for it, or I'd just go crazy thinking about it. Like I said, I have to walk or starve or... whatever. I always do something. It's like a rule in my head. Speaking of rules, the owner at the cleaners just put up this new sign about breaks. We used to be able to just take our fifteen minutes whenever things were slow, but now he wants us to clock out exactly at 10:15 and 2:15. It's so rigid. And the time clock is this ancient punch-card thing that jams half the time. My coworker, Rosa—she's the one who does the alterations—she actually got her cardigan sleeve caught in it last week. It was a nice cardigan, too, this really pretty mustard yellow. She said she got it on clearance at that department store that's closing down in the mall. I haven't been to the mall in ages. The parking is just a nightmare, especially near the food court entrance. But yeah, no, I never just eat all that and do nothing. I always have to fix it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you ate a large amount of food in a short time without doing anything afterwards to try to get rid of the calories?\n\nPatient: Are you... are you serious right now? I feel like I'm taking crazy pills. I just told you, like, three times. No. Never. I *always* have to do something. I can't just let it sit there. \n\nIt's like you're not even listening to me. Wait, didn't you literally just ask me that? Like, two seconds ago? I swear, I just told you I don't know. I really don't know what causes it. It just happens. 

You're honestly worse than Mr. Henderson at the shop. He comes in every single Tuesday with these hideous plaid button-downs—like, lumberjack meets 1970s basement couch—and he always asks, "Now, you're going to use the heavy starch on the collars, right?" And every single week, I tell him, "Mr. Henderson, we don't do heavy starch here, we use the light liquid kind." And he just nods and says, "Good, good." It's like his brain just resets every seven days. His wife usually waits out in the car while he drops them off. It's this huge boat of a Buick, powder blue, with the fake wood paneling on the inside. One time she left the headlights on and the battery died right in our loading zone. The tow truck took like three hours to get there because it was pouring rain. I hate the rain, honestly. It makes all the wool coats smell like wet dog when people bring them in, and the humidity messes with the steam presses. 

But seriously... I don't have a different answer for you than the one I just gave. I don't know why I lose control. I just do.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess what brings me in is... I keep making myself throw up after I eat. It's getting really bad. Sometimes I even do it in the bathroom at work, which is so risky because the machines are loud, but if someone walks in, you know? I work at this big commercial laundry place, and the steam presses are always hissing, so that helps cover the noise, but still. Or I'll do it right when I get home. My neighbor's dog is always barking when I get home, this little terrier mix that just yaps at every single car that drives by. It drives me crazy, honestly. But yeah, the throwing up... I hate it, I really do, but it’s started feeling like the only way I can calm down.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... it's a lot. For months now, I'd say most weeks have at least a couple of times where I just lose control and eat way more than I ever meant to. And then I have to fix it, you know? So I throw up, or I just don't eat at all the next day, or I try to walk it off for hours. I actually walk down by the old canal when I do that. It's supposed to be this nice walking path now, the city spent all this money putting in these benches and those little solar lights, but half the lights are already broken. Kids come down there and smash them, I think. I saw a group of teenagers down there last Tuesday just hanging out, smoking, and I thought about saying something, but you never know these days, right? You say the wrong thing and suddenly you're the bad guy. But yeah, the eating and then trying to undo it... it's at least a couple times a week, sometimes more.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, god, yes. I'm terrified of it. It's like... if the scale goes up even a tiny bit, or if my waistband feels even a little tight under my work apron, I just panic. I feel like I've ruined everything and I'm going to be completely fat by the end of the day. Speaking of my apron, they just switched our uniform supplier at work, and the new ones are this awful stiff material. They don't breathe at all, which is terrible when you're working around those giant industrial dryers all day. The old ones were much softer, kind of a cotton blend, I think. I asked my manager, Dave, if we could go back to the old ones, but he said corporate signed a two-year contract, so we're stuck with them. Dave's nice enough, but he never really stands up to corporate, you know? He just kind of nods and does whatever they say. But yeah, the fear of gaining weight... it's always there. It's exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without throwing up afterwards?\n\nPatient: Well, no... I mean, I always end up having to fix it afterwards. But when I do start eating, it's like I can't stop. I'll go through a whole family-size bag of chips, and then maybe some pastries I picked up from the corner store on my way home. The guy who runs that store, Mr. Patel, he's always so friendly, always asks about my day. He's got this old cash register that makes this loud *ding* sound every time he opens it, it's almost vintage, you know? I think he said his grandfather bought it back in the seventies or something. But yeah, I'll eat the pastries, and then whatever leftovers are in the fridge, and wash it all down with a bunch of sweet drinks... all in one sitting. It's like I don't even realize how much I've eaten until it's all gone and the wrappers are just sitting there. And then the panic sets in, and I have to go throw it up.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the main reason you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... it's the main reason, definitely. I just can't stand the thought of getting bigger. It's like this voice in my head that just won't shut up about it. Just like my neighbor's dog, actually. They got this little terrier mix a few weeks ago, and it barks at absolutely everything. A leaf blows across the sidewalk? Barking. The mail carrier comes? Nonstop barking. I tried talking to them about it, they live in 4B, the couple with the weird green car... it's like a neon green, you can't miss it in the parking lot. Anyway, I asked if they could maybe keep the dog inside during the day when they're at work, but they said he has separation anxiety. Which, I get it, animals have feelings too, but it's driving me crazy when I'm trying to sleep after a long shift. But yeah, the fear of gaining weight... that's why I do it. I just have to get it out before it turns into fat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating these large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Once I start eating, it feels like my hands just keep reaching for food even while I'm telling myself to stop. It's like I'm watching myself do it from the outside and I just cannot make myself quit. It's the weirdest feeling, almost like being on autopilot. Speaking of autopilot, my car has been doing this strange thing lately where the cruise control won't turn off right away when I tap the brakes. It's a 2008 Honda, so it's getting up there in miles. I took it to my mechanic, Luis—he's got this shop down by the old textile mill—and he said it might be a sensor issue. Luis is great, though. He always has these little hard candies on his counter, the strawberry ones with the gooey centers? I love those. He said his wife buys them in bulk from some warehouse club. I should probably ask him which one, because they're really good. But yeah, with the food... I just can't stop once I start. There's no control at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time and then not thrown up afterwards?\n\nPatient: Well, sometimes... I mean, if I don't throw up, I still have to do something about it, right? So on the days after I eat way too much and don't get sick, I try to make up for it by just not eating anything at all until really late at night. Or I'll force myself to walk for miles and exercise, even when my legs are absolutely throbbing from standing at the cleaners all day. \n\nWorking the steam press is just brutal on your feet. I actually bought these memory foam inserts for my sneakers recently, the ones they advertise on TV? The commercial has this guy dropping a bowling ball on the foam, which makes zero sense when you actually think about it. Like, who is dropping bowling balls on their shoes? But I bought them anyway at the pharmacy next to the dry cleaners. The pharmacist there, Brenda, she always wears these wild earrings. Yesterday she had on these little miniature flamingos. I asked her where she gets them, and she said she makes them herself out of polymer clay in her oven. I tried getting into crafts once, knitting mostly, but I just ended up with a bunch of tangled yarn in a garbage bag. \n\nBut yeah... if I don't get rid of the food that way, I just starve myself the next day or walk until I can barely feel my feet. I just have to make up for it somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a very large amount of food in a short time and then not done anything to try to get rid of the calories?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I always have to do *something*. If I didn't, I'd just sit there feeling it all turning into fat, and I can't handle that. It's like this heavy, awful feeling. Actually, speaking of heavy, you wouldn't believe the bags of laundry some people bring in. Just yesterday, this guy brought in a duffel bag—one of those massive military-style ones—completely stuffed with wool sweaters. Who wears that many wool sweaters in this weather? And they smell like mothballs, which is just the worst smell. It gets right up into your sinuses and stays there all day. I tried drinking peppermint tea to clear it out, because my aunt used to say peppermint cures everything. She used to grow it in her backyard in these old coffee cans. Folgers, I think. The red plastic ones. She'd paint them with these little daisies. She was so artistic like that, not like me with my tangled knitting yarn. But no, to answer your question, I never just let it sit there. I always have to get rid of it or work it off somehow. I just have to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without doing anything to try to get rid of the calories afterwards?\n\nPatient: I just... didn't I just say that? No, I never do that. I always have to do something to make up for it, or I'd just go crazy thinking about it. Like I said, I have to walk or starve or... whatever. I always do something. It's like a rule in my head. Speaking of rules, the owner at the cleaners just put up this new sign about breaks. We used to be able to just take our fifteen minutes whenever things were slow, but now he wants us to clock out exactly at 10:15 and 2:15. It's so rigid. And the time clock is this ancient punch-card thing that jams half the time. My coworker, Rosa—she's the one who does the alterations—she actually got her cardigan sleeve caught in it last week. It was a nice cardigan, too, this really pretty mustard yellow. She said she got it on clearance at that department store that's closing down in the mall. I haven't been to the mall in ages. The parking is just a nightmare, especially near the food court entrance. But yeah, no, I never just eat all that and do nothing. I always have to fix it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you ate a large amount of food in a short time without doing anything afterwards to try to get rid of the calories?\n\nPatient: Are you... are you serious right now? I feel like I'm taking crazy pills. I just told you, like, three times. No. Never. I *always* have to do something. I can't just let it sit there. \n\nIt's like you're not even listening to me. My ex-boyfriend used to do that. Gary. You'd be talking right to his face and his eyes would just glaze over like he was watching a TV show behind my forehead. He was a mechanic, always had grease under his fingernails no matter how much he scrubbed. He used this orange pumice soap that smelled like fake citrus. I hate that smell. It reminds me of the bathroom cleaner they use at the bus station. I had to take the bus for a month when my car broke down—the alternator went out. It was a nightmare. The bus driver was this guy who always whistled show tunes. *Phantom of the Opera*, mostly. \n\nBut seriously, doc, how many times are you going to ask me the exact same thing? It actually reminds me of the CD player we have at the front counter at the cleaners. The laser must be dirty or scratched or something because it always skips on track four. It's this old Michael Bublé Christmas album that my manager refuses to take out of the machine, even though it's not even close to the holidays. It just skips and repeats the exact same line over and over. It drives me absolutely up the wall. I tried to clean it last week with one of those special discs, you know the ones with the tiny little brushes glued to the bottom? But I think I just made it worse. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Are you... are you actually kidding me right now? You're asking me the exact same thing for the third time! I don't know why you're not hearing me, but no, I don't know if the weight thing is what starts it. What I *do* know is that once I start eating, it feels like my hands just have a mind of their own. They just keep reaching for more food, even when my brain is screaming at me to stop. It's like I'm standing outside my own body, just watching myself shove food in my mouth, and I absolutely cannot make myself quit. 

It's actually a lot like watching Rosa try to fix the hem on Mrs. Gable's silk dresses. Mrs. Gable is this incredibly picky lady who lives over in the gated community by the golf course—the one with the massive stone lions out front. Anyway, Rosa always thinks she can just eyeball the hem, and I'm standing right there telling her, "Rosa, use the measuring tape, you're going to make it crooked." And I just have to stand there and watch her ruin it, knowing I can't stop her because she's technically been there two years longer than me. Last time she messed up, Mrs. Gable threw an absolute fit. She had this tiny little rhinestone purse that she kept swinging around while she yelled, and one of the rhinestones actually flew off and hit the front window. I found it when I was sweeping up later. I actually kept it. I put it in the little cup holder in my car, right next to all the spare change I use for the toll booth on Route 9. They just raised the toll again, by the way. It's ridiculous. I don't even know where that money goes because the potholes on that road are big enough to swallow a tire. 

But yeah, when I'm eating, I just can't stop. I'm just watching myself do it. I don't know what else to tell you.
