I asked my manager, Dave, if we could go back to the old ones, but he said corporate signed a two-year contract, so we're stuck with them. Dave's nice enough, but he never really stands up to corporate, you know? He just kind of nods and does whatever they say. But yeah, the fear of gaining weight... it's always there. It's exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without throwing up afterwards?\n\nPatient: Well, no... I mean, I always end up having to fix it afterwards. But when I do start eating, it's like I can't stop. I'll go through a whole family-size bag of chips, and then maybe some pastries I picked up from the corner store on my way home. The guy who runs that store, Mr. Patel, he's always so friendly, always asks about my day. He's got this old cash register that makes this loud *ding* sound every time he opens it, it's almost vintage, you know? I think he said his grandfather bought it back in the seventies or something. But yeah, I'll eat the pastries, and then whatever leftovers are in the fridge, and wash it all down with a bunch of sweet drinks... all in one sitting. It's like I don't even realize how much I've eaten until it's all gone and the wrappers are just sitting there. And then the panic sets in, and I have to go throw it up.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the main reason you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... it's the main reason, definitely. I just can't stand the thought of getting bigger. It's like this voice in my head that just won't shut up about it. Just like my neighbor's dog, actually. They got this little terrier mix a few weeks ago, and it barks at absolutely everything. A leaf blows across the sidewalk? Barking. The mail carrier comes? Nonstop barking. I tried talking to them about it, they live in 4B, the couple with the weird green car... it's like a neon green, you can't miss it in the parking lot. Anyway, I asked if they could maybe keep the dog inside during the day when they're at work, but they said he has separation anxiety. Which, I get it, animals have feelings too, but it's driving me crazy when I'm trying to sleep after a long shift. But yeah, the fear of gaining weight... that's why I do it. I just have to get it out before it turns into fat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating these large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Once I start eating, it feels like my hands just keep reaching for food even while I'm telling myself to stop. It's like I'm watching myself do it from the outside and I just cannot make myself quit. It's the weirdest feeling, almost like being on autopilot. Speaking of autopilot, my car has been doing this strange thing lately where the cruise control won't turn off right away when I tap the brakes. It's a 2008 Honda, so it's getting up there in miles. I took it to my mechanic, Luis—he's got this shop down by the old textile mill—and he said it might be a sensor issue. Luis is great, though. He always has these little hard candies on his counter, the strawberry ones with the gooey centers? I love those. He said his wife buys them in bulk from some warehouse club. I should probably ask him which one, because they're really good. But yeah, with the food... I just can't stop once I start. There's no control at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time and then not thrown up afterwards?\n\nPatient: Well, sometimes... I mean, if I don't throw up, I still have to do something about it, right? So on the days after I eat way too much and don't get sick, I try to make up for it by just not eating anything at all until really late at night. Or I'll force myself to walk for miles and exercise, even when my legs are absolutely throbbing from standing at the cleaners all day. \n\nWorking the steam press is just brutal on your feet. I actually bought these memory foam inserts for my sneakers recently, the ones they advertise on TV? The commercial has this guy dropping a bowling ball on the foam, which makes zero sense when you actually think about it. Like, who is dropping bowling balls on their shoes? But I bought them anyway at the pharmacy next to the dry cleaners. The pharmacist there, Brenda, she always wears these wild earrings. Yesterday she had on these little miniature flamingos. I asked her where she gets them, and she said she makes them herself out of polymer clay in her oven. I tried getting into crafts once, knitting mostly, but I just ended up with a bunch of tangled yarn in a garbage bag. \n\nBut yeah... if I don't get rid of the food that way, I just starve myself the next day or walk until I can barely feel my feet. I just have to make up for it somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a very large amount of food in a short time and then not done anything to try to get rid of the calories?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I always have to do *something*. If I didn't, I'd just sit there feeling it all turning into fat, and I can't handle that. It's like this heavy, awful feeling. Actually, speaking of heavy, you wouldn't believe the bags of laundry some people bring in. Just yesterday, this guy brought in a duffel bag—one of those massive military-style ones—completely stuffed with wool sweaters. Who wears that many wool sweaters in this weather? And they smell like mothballs, which is just the worst smell. It gets right up into your sinuses and stays there all day. I tried drinking peppermint tea to clear it out, because my aunt used to say peppermint cures everything. She used to grow it in her backyard in these old coffee cans. Folgers, I think. The red plastic ones. She'd paint them with these little daisies. She was so artistic like that, not like me with my tangled knitting yarn. But no, to answer your question, I never just let it sit there. I always have to get rid of it or work it off somehow. I just have to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without doing anything to try to get rid of the calories afterwards?\n\nPatient: I just... didn't I just say that? No, I never do that. I always have to do something to make up for it, or I'd just go crazy thinking about it. Like I said, I have to walk or starve or... whatever. I always do something. It's like a rule in my head. Speaking of rules, the owner at the cleaners just put up this new sign about breaks. We used to be able to just take our fifteen minutes whenever things were slow, but now he wants us to clock out exactly at 10:15 and 2:15. It's so rigid. And the time clock is this ancient punch-card thing that jams half the time. My coworker, Rosa—she's the one who does the alterations—she actually got her cardigan sleeve caught in it last week. It was a nice cardigan, too, this really pretty mustard yellow. She said she got it on clearance at that department store that's closing down in the mall. I haven't been to the mall in ages. The parking is just a nightmare, especially near the food court entrance. But yeah, no, I never just eat all that and do nothing. I always have to fix it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you ate a large amount of food in a short time without doing anything afterwards to try to get rid of the calories?\n\nPatient: Are you... are you serious right now? I feel like I'm taking crazy pills. I just told you, like, three times. No. Never. I *always* have to do something. I can't just let it sit there. \n\nIt's like you're not even listening to me. My ex-boyfriend used to do that. Gary. You'd be talking right to his face and his eyes would just glaze over like he was watching a TV show behind my forehead. He was a mechanic, always had grease under his fingernails no matter how much he scrubbed. He used this orange pumice soap that smelled like fake citrus. I hate that smell. It reminds me of the bathroom cleaner they use at the bus station. I had to take the bus for a month when my car broke down—the alternator went out. It was a nightmare. The bus driver was this guy who always whistled show tunes. *Phantom of the Opera*, mostly. \n\nBut seriously, doc, how many times are you going to ask me the exact same thing? The answer is no, I never just do nothing. I always fix it. Always.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any instance where you ate a large amount of food in a short time and then didn't throw up, exercise, or restrict your food intake afterwards?\n\nPatient: I just... I honestly don't know how else to say it to you. No. There is no instance. Not one. I never, ever just eat all that and do nothing. \n\nYou're like a broken record, you know that? It actually reminds me of the CD player we have at the front counter at the cleaners. The laser must be dirty or scratched or something because it always skips on track four. It's this old Michael Bublé Christmas album that my manager refuses to take out of the machine, even though it's not even close to the holidays. It just skips and repeats the exact same line over and over. It drives me absolutely up the wall. I tried to clean it last week with one of those special discs, you know the ones with the tiny little brushes glued to the bottom? But I think I just made it worse. Rosa told me I should just accidentally knock the whole stereo into the trash can. Honestly, I'm tempted. Though our dumpsters out back smell so bad right now I don't even want to go near them. The seafood place next door keeps leaking their old frying oil into the alley, and the stray cats come and lick it up. It's so gross. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question again, no. I never do that. I always have to get rid of it.\n\nDoctor: Is it the fear of gaining weight that triggers your loss of control when eating?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't really know if it's the fear of gaining weight that starts it. I mean, I am terrified of gaining weight, obviously, but when I lose control, it's just... I'm not sure what triggers it exactly. It just happens. \n\nSpeaking of triggers, the bell above the door at the cleaners is driving me insane lately. Every time someone walks in, it does this really high-pitched jingle. It didn't used to bother me, but the spring is loose or something, so now it rattles for like ten seconds after the door closes. It sets my teeth on edge. Rosa says it sounds like a dog with a rusty collar scratching itself. She actually has two dogs, pugs, I think? Or maybe French bulldogs. One of them has this breathing problem where it snores even when it's awake. She showed me a video on her phone, and the screen was completely cracked. She said she dropped it in the parking lot at the grocery store. The one on Elm Street, you know? They just remodeled the produce section, but they moved all the apples to the other side, and it's so confusing. I spent twenty minutes looking for Granny Smiths last time I was there. \n\nBut yeah, I don't really know what starts the eating. I just know I can't stop once I do.\n\nDoctor: Is the fear of gaining weight what causes you to lose control when you're eating?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you literally just ask me that? Like, two seconds ago? I swear, I just told you I don't know. I really don't know what causes it. It just happens. \n\nYou're honestly worse than Mr. Henderson at the shop. He comes in every single Tuesday with these hideous plaid button-downs—like, lumberjack meets 1970s basement couch—and he always asks, \"Now, you're going to use the heavy starch on the collars, right?\" And every single week, I tell him, \"Mr. Henderson, we don't do heavy starch here, we use the light liquid kind.\" And he just nods and says, \"Good, good.\" It's like his brain just resets every seven days. His wife usually waits out in the car while he drops them off. It's this huge boat of a Buick, powder blue, with the fake wood paneling on the inside. One time she left the headlights on and the battery died right in our loading zone. The tow truck took like three hours to get there because it was pouring rain. I hate the rain, honestly. It makes all the wool coats smell like wet dog when people bring them in, and the humidity messes with the steam presses. \n\nBut seriously... I don't have a different answer for you than the one I just gave. I don't know why I lose control. I just do.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the primary reason you feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Are you... are you actually kidding me right now? You're asking me the exact same thing for the third time! I don't know why you're not hearing me, but no, I don't know if the weight thing is what starts it. What I *do* know is that once I start eating, it feels like my hands just have a mind of their own. They just keep reaching for more food, even when my brain is screaming at me to stop. It's like I'm standing outside my own body, just watching myself shove food in my mouth, and I absolutely cannot make myself quit. \n\nIt's actually a lot like watching Rosa try to fix the hem on Mrs. Gable's silk dresses. Mrs. Gable is this incredibly picky lady who lives over in the gated community by the golf course—the one with the massive stone lions out front. Anyway, Rosa always thinks she can just eyeball the hem, and I'm standing right there telling her, \"Rosa, use the measuring tape, you're going to make it crooked.\" And I just have to stand there and watch her ruin it, knowing I can't stop her because she's technically been there two years longer than me. Last time she messed up, Mrs. Gable threw an absolute fit. She had this tiny little rhinestone purse that she kept swinging around while she yelled, and one of the rhinestones actually flew off and hit the front window. I found it when I was sweeping up later. I actually kept it. I put it in the little cup holder in my car, right next to all the spare change I use for the toll booth on Route 9. They just raised the toll again, by the way. It's ridiculous. I don't even know where that money goes because the potholes on that road are big enough to swallow a tire. \n\nBut yeah, when I'm eating, I just can't stop. I'm just watching myself do it. I don't know what else to tell you.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Are you actually messing with me right now? That is the fourth time. You've asked me the exact same thing four times! 

Look, I don't know if it's the trigger for the eating, like I've been saying. But am I afraid of gaining weight? Yes. If the scale goes up even a little bit, or if my waistband feels tight under my work apron, I completely panic. It feels like I have ruined everything and I will be fat by the end of the day. 

And honestly, those work aprons do not help anything. The owner just switched to a new supplier, and they're made of this horrible stiff polyester that doesn't breathe at all. Plus, they're this ugly maroon color now. They used to be navy blue, which hid the spot-treatment chemicals way better. Now if I splash a little of the solvent on myself, it leaves this weird dark ring that looks like a grease stain. The solvent smells terrible, too. It gets in your hair and you absolutely cannot wash it out. I buy this really expensive coconut shampoo from the drugstore on the corner—the one where the automatic doors are always broken, so you have to pry them open like you're breaking in—and even that doesn't get the smell out. I was complaining to the cashier about it yesterday. Her name is Brenda, and she has these really long acrylic nails painted like little watermelons. I have no idea how she types on the register with those things. I'd be hitting three buttons at once. 

But anyway, yeah. I panic about my weight all the time. I just don't know if that's what makes me lose control with the food. Can we please, please move on to a different question now?

I tried getting into crafts once, knitting mostly, but I just ended up with a bunch of tangled yarn in a garbage bag. \n\nBut yeah... if I don't get rid of the food that way, I just starve myself the next day or walk until I can barely feel my feet. I just have to make up for it somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a very large amount of food in a short time and then not done anything to try to get rid of the calories?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I always have to do *something*. If I didn't, I'd just sit there feeling it all turning into fat, and I can't handle that. It's like this heavy, awful feeling. Actually, speaking of heavy, you wouldn't believe the bags of laundry some people bring in. Just yesterday, this guy brought in a duffel bag—one of those massive military-style ones—completely stuffed with wool sweaters. Who wears that many wool sweaters in this weather? And they smell like mothballs, which is just the worst smell. It gets right up into your sinuses and stays there all day. I tried drinking peppermint tea to clear it out, because my aunt used to say peppermint cures everything. She used to grow it in her backyard in these old coffee cans. Folgers, I think. The red plastic ones. She'd paint them with these little daisies. She was so artistic like that, not like me with my tangled knitting yarn. But no, to answer your question, I never just let it sit there. I always have to get rid of it or work it off somehow. I just have to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without doing anything to try to get rid of the calories afterwards?\n\nPatient: I just... didn't I just say that? No, I never do that. I always have to do something to make up for it, or I'd just go crazy thinking about it. Like I said, I have to walk or starve or... whatever. I always do something. It's like a rule in my head. Speaking of rules, the owner at the cleaners just put up this new sign about breaks. We used to be able to just take our fifteen minutes whenever things were slow, but now he wants us to clock out exactly at 10:15 and 2:15. It's so rigid. And the time clock is this ancient punch-card thing that jams half the time. My coworker, Rosa—she's the one who does the alterations—she actually got her cardigan sleeve caught in it last week. It was a nice cardigan, too, this really pretty mustard yellow. She said she got it on clearance at that department store that's closing down in the mall. I haven't been to the mall in ages. The parking is just a nightmare, especially near the food court entrance. But yeah, no, I never just eat all that and do nothing. I always have to fix it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you ate a large amount of food in a short time without doing anything afterwards to try to get rid of the calories?\n\nPatient: Are you... are you serious right now? I feel like I'm taking crazy pills. I just told you, like, three times. No. Never. I *always* have to do something. I can't just let it sit there. \n\nIt's like you're not even listening to me. My ex-boyfriend used to do that. Gary. You'd be talking right to his face and his eyes would just glaze over like he was watching a TV show behind my forehead. He was a mechanic, always had grease under his fingernails no matter how much he scrubbed. He used this orange pumice soap that smelled like fake citrus. I hate that smell. It reminds me of the bathroom cleaner they use at the bus station. I had to take the bus for a month when my car broke down—the alternator went out. It was a nightmare. The bus driver was this guy who always whistled show tunes. *Phantom of the Opera*, mostly. \n\nBut seriously, doc, how many times are you going to ask me the exact same thing? The answer is no, I never just do nothing. I always fix it. Always.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any instance where you ate a large amount of food in a short time and then didn't throw up, exercise, or restrict your food intake afterwards?\n\nPatient: I just... I honestly don't know how else to say it to you. No. There is no instance. Not one. I never, ever just eat all that and do nothing. \n\nYou're like a broken record, you know that? It actually reminds me of the CD player we have at the front counter at the cleaners. The laser must be dirty or scratched or something because it always skips on track four. It's this old Michael Bublé Christmas album that my manager refuses to take out of the machine, even though it's not even close to the holidays. It just skips and repeats the exact same line over and over. It drives me absolutely up the wall. I tried to clean it last week with one of those special discs, you know the ones with the tiny little brushes glued to the bottom? But I think I just made it worse. Rosa told me I should just accidentally knock the whole stereo into the trash can. Honestly, I'm tempted. Though our dumpsters out back smell so bad right now I don't even want to go near them. The seafood place next door keeps leaking their old frying oil into the alley, and the stray cats come and lick it up. It's so gross. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question again, no. I never do that. I always have to get rid of it.\n\nDoctor: Is it the fear of gaining weight that triggers your loss of control when eating?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't really know if it's the fear of gaining weight that starts it. I mean, I am terrified of gaining weight, obviously, but when I lose control, it's just... I'm not sure what triggers it exactly. It just happens. \n\nSpeaking of triggers, the bell above the door at the cleaners is driving me insane lately. Every time someone walks in, it does this really high-pitched jingle. It didn't used to bother me, but the spring is loose or something, so now it rattles for like ten seconds after the door closes. It sets my teeth on edge. Rosa says it sounds like a dog with a rusty collar scratching itself. She actually has two dogs, pugs, I think? Or maybe French bulldogs. One of them has this breathing problem where it snores even when it's awake. She showed me a video on her phone, and the screen was completely cracked. She said she dropped it in the parking lot at the grocery store. The one on Elm Street, you know? They just remodeled the produce section, but they moved all the apples to the other side, and it's so confusing. I spent twenty minutes looking for Granny Smiths last time I was there. \n\nBut yeah, I don't really know what starts the eating. I just know I can't stop once I do.\n\nDoctor: Is the fear of gaining weight what causes you to lose control when you're eating?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you literally just ask me that? Like, two seconds ago? I swear, I just told you I don't know. I really don't know what causes it. It just happens. \n\nYou're honestly worse than Mr. Henderson at the shop. He comes in every single Tuesday with these hideous plaid button-downs—like, lumberjack meets 1970s basement couch—and he always asks, \"Now, you're going to use the heavy starch on the collars, right?\" And every single week, I tell him, \"Mr. Henderson, we don't do heavy starch here, we use the light liquid kind.\" And he just nods and says, \"Good, good.\" It's like his brain just resets every seven days. His wife usually waits out in the car while he drops them off. It's this huge boat of a Buick, powder blue, with the fake wood paneling on the inside. One time she left the headlights on and the battery died right in our loading zone. The tow truck took like three hours to get there because it was pouring rain. I hate the rain, honestly. It makes all the wool coats smell like wet dog when people bring them in, and the humidity messes with the steam presses. \n\nBut seriously... I don't have a different answer for you than the one I just gave. I don't know why I lose control. I just do.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the primary reason you feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Are you... are you actually kidding me right now? You're asking me the exact same thing for the third time! I don't know why you're not hearing me, but no, I don't know if the weight thing is what starts it. What I *do* know is that once I start eating, it feels like my hands just have a mind of their own. They just keep reaching for more food, even when my brain is screaming at me to stop. It's like I'm standing outside my own body, just watching myself shove food in my mouth, and I absolutely cannot make myself quit. \n\nIt's actually a lot like watching Rosa try to fix the hem on Mrs. Gable's silk dresses. Mrs. Gable is this incredibly picky lady who lives over in the gated community by the golf course—the one with the massive stone lions out front. Anyway, Rosa always thinks she can just eyeball the hem, and I'm standing right there telling her, \"Rosa, use the measuring tape, you're going to make it crooked.\" And I just have to stand there and watch her ruin it, knowing I can't stop her because she's technically been there two years longer than me. Last time she messed up, Mrs. Gable threw an absolute fit. She had this tiny little rhinestone purse that she kept swinging around while she yelled, and one of the rhinestones actually flew off and hit the front window. I found it when I was sweeping up later. I actually kept it. I put it in the little cup holder in my car, right next to all the spare change I use for the toll booth on Route 9. They just raised the toll again, by the way. It's ridiculous. I don't even know where that money goes because the potholes on that road are big enough to swallow a tire. \n\nBut yeah, when I'm eating, I just can't stop. I'm just watching myself do it. I don't know what else to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight what makes you feel like you're losing control when you eat?\n\nPatient: Are you actually messing with me right now? That is the fourth time. You've asked me the exact same thing four times! \n\nLook, I don't know if it's the trigger for the eating, like I've been saying. But am I afraid of gaining weight? Yes. If the scale goes up even a little bit, or if my waistband feels tight under my work apron, I completely panic. It feels like I have ruined everything and I will be fat by the end of the day. \n\nAnd honestly, those work aprons do not help anything. The owner just switched to a new supplier, and they're made of this horrible stiff polyester that doesn't breathe at all. Plus, they're this ugly maroon color now. They used to be navy blue, which hid the spot-treatment chemicals way better. Now if I splash a little of the solvent on myself, it leaves this weird dark ring that looks like a grease stain. The solvent smells terrible, too. It gets in your hair and you absolutely cannot wash it out. I buy this really expensive coconut shampoo from the drugstore on the corner—the one where the automatic doors are always broken, so you have to pry them open like you're breaking in—and even that doesn't get the smell out. I was complaining to the cashier about it yesterday. Her name is Brenda, and she has these really long acrylic nails painted like little watermelons. I have no idea how she types on the register with those things. I'd be hitting three buttons at once. \n\nBut anyway, yeah. I panic about my weight all the time. I just don't know if that's what makes me lose control with the food. Can we please, please move on to a different question now?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Okay, seriously, are you a robot? Is this a hidden camera show? Because that is literally the fifth time you have asked me some version of that exact same question! 

I do not know if the fear of gaining weight is what drives it! I really don't. All I know is that when it happens, I eat so much stuff. I can go through a full bag of chips, a bunch of pastries from the corner store, whatever leftovers are sitting in the fridge, and chug a bunch of sweet drinks all in one sitting. Half the time, I don't even really notice how much is gone until it's over and I'm just looking at empty wrappers. 

And speaking of those pastries from the corner store, the guy who runs the place—Sal—he is always trying to sell me the day-old ones at full price. He thinks I don't notice, but the icing gets all crusty and flakes off everywhere when they sit out. Plus, he keeps this giant, fat orange cat in the store that just sleeps right on top of the scratch-off lottery machine. It sheds hair everywhere. I don't even buy lottery tickets anyway. My Uncle Gary used to buy them every single morning. He actually won five hundred bucks once, but then he just turned around and spent it all on more tickets and lost every single penny. He ended up living in this beat-up RV parked behind a diner for like three years after that. The diner had really good pancakes, though. They used actual real buttermilk, not that cheap powdered stuff they used to serve us in the cafeteria back in high school. I absolutely hated high school lunches. The meatloaf always bounced if you dropped it. That's probably half the reason I dropped out in tenth grade, honestly, the food was basically toxic waste. 

But anyway, yes, I eat a massive amount of food really fast before I even realize it. Can we *please* talk about something else now? Because if you ask me about my fear of gaining weight one more time, I think I'm going to scream.

I should probably ask him which one, because they're really good. But yeah, with the food... I just can't stop once I start. His wife usually waits out in the car while he drops them off. It's this huge boat of a Buick, powder blue, with the fake wood paneling on the inside. One time she left the headlights on and the battery died right in our loading zone. The tow truck took like three hours to get there because it was pouring rain. I hate the rain, honestly. It makes all the wool coats smell like wet dog when people bring them in, and the humidity messes with the steam presses. \n\nBut seriously... I don't have a different answer for you than the one I just gave. I don't know why I lose control. You're asking me the exact same thing for the third time! I don't know why you're not hearing me, but no, I don't know if the weight thing is what starts it. What I *do* know is that once I start eating, it feels like my hands just have a mind of their own. They just keep reaching for more food, even when my brain is screaming at me to stop. It's like I'm standing outside my own body, just watching myself shove food in my mouth, and I absolutely cannot make myself quit. \n\nIt's actually a lot like watching Rosa try to fix the hem on Mrs. Gable's silk dresses. Mrs. Gable is this incredibly picky lady who lives over in the gated community by the golf course—the one with the massive stone lions out front. Anyway, Rosa always thinks she can just eyeball the hem, and I'm standing right there telling her, \"Rosa, use the measuring tape, you're going to make it crooked.\" And I just have to stand there and watch her ruin it, knowing I can't stop her because she's technically been there two years longer than me. Last time she messed up, Mrs. Gable threw an absolute fit. She had this tiny little rhinestone purse that she kept swinging around while she yelled, and one of the rhinestones actually flew off and hit the front window. I found it when I was sweeping up later. I actually kept it. I put it in the little cup holder in my car, right next to all the spare change I use for the toll booth on Route 9. They just raised the toll again, by the way. It's ridiculous. I don't even know where that money goes because the potholes on that road are big enough to swallow a tire. \n\nBut yeah, when I'm eating, I just can't stop. I'm just watching myself do it. I don't know what else to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight what makes you feel like you're losing control when you eat?\n\nPatient: Are you actually messing with me right now? That is the fourth time. You've asked me the exact same thing four times! \n\nLook, I don't know if it's the trigger for the eating, like I've been saying. But am I afraid of gaining weight? Yes. If the scale goes up even a little bit, or if my waistband feels tight under my work apron, I completely panic. It feels like I have ruined everything and I will be fat by the end of the day. \n\nAnd honestly, those work aprons do not help anything. The owner just switched to a new supplier, and they're made of this horrible stiff polyester that doesn't breathe at all. Plus, they're this ugly maroon color now. They used to be navy blue, which hid the spot-treatment chemicals way better. Now if I splash a little of the solvent on myself, it leaves this weird dark ring that looks like a grease stain. The solvent smells terrible, too. It gets in your hair and you absolutely cannot wash it out. I buy this really expensive coconut shampoo from the drugstore on the corner—the one where the automatic doors are always broken, so you have to pry them open like you're breaking in—and even that doesn't get the smell out. I was complaining to the cashier about it yesterday. Her name is Brenda, and she has these really long acrylic nails painted like little watermelons. I have no idea how she types on the register with those things. I'd be hitting three buttons at once. \n\nBut anyway, yeah. I panic about my weight all the time. I just don't know if that's what makes me lose control with the food. Can we please, please move on to a different question now?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is what drives you to eat large amounts of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Okay, seriously, are you a robot? Just yesterday, this guy brought in a duffel bag—one of those massive military-style ones—completely stuffed with wool sweaters. Who wears that many wool sweaters in this weather? And they smell like mothballs, which is just the worst smell. It gets right up into your sinuses and stays there all day. I tried drinking peppermint tea to clear it out, because my aunt used to say peppermint cures everything. She used to grow it in her backyard in these old coffee cans. Folgers, I think. The red plastic ones. She'd paint them with these little daisies. She was so artistic like that, not like me with my tangled knitting yarn. But no, to answer your question, I never just let it sit there. I always have to get rid of it or work it off somehow. I just have to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without doing anything to try to get rid of the calories afterwards?\n\nPatient: I just... didn't I just say that? No, I never do that. I always have to do something to make up for it, or I'd just go crazy thinking about it. Like I said, I have to walk or starve or... whatever. I always do something. It's like a rule in my head. Speaking of rules, the owner at the cleaners just put up this new sign about breaks. We used to be able to just take our fifteen minutes whenever things were slow, but now he wants us to clock out exactly at 10:15 and 2:15. It's so rigid. And the time clock is this ancient punch-card thing that jams half the time. My coworker, Rosa—she's the one who does the alterations—she actually got her cardigan sleeve caught in it last week. It was a nice cardigan, too, this really pretty mustard yellow. She said she got it on clearance at that department store that's closing down in the mall. I haven't been to the mall in ages. The parking is just a nightmare, especially near the food court entrance. But yeah, no, I never just eat all that and do nothing. I always have to fix it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you ate a large amount of food in a short time without doing anything afterwards to try to get rid of the calories?\n\nPatient: Are you... are you serious right now? I feel like I'm taking crazy pills. I just told you, like, three times. No. Never. I *always* have to do something. I can't just let it sit there. \n\nIt's like you're not even listening to me. My ex-boyfriend used to do that. Gary. You'd be talking right to his face and his eyes would just glaze over like he was watching a TV show behind my forehead. He was a mechanic, always had grease under his fingernails no matter how much he scrubbed. He used this orange pumice soap that smelled like fake citrus. I hate that smell. It reminds me of the bathroom cleaner they use at the bus station. I had to take the bus for a month when my car broke down—the alternator went out. It was a nightmare. The bus driver was this guy who always whistled show tunes. *Phantom of the Opera*, mostly. \n\nBut seriously, doc, how many times are you going to ask me the exact same thing? The answer is no, I never just do nothing. I always fix it. Always.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any instance where you ate a large amount of food in a short time and then didn't throw up, exercise, or restrict your food intake afterwards?\n\nPatient: I just... I honestly don't know how else to say it to you. No. There is no instance. Not one. I never, ever just eat all that and do nothing. \n\nYou're like a broken record, you know that? It actually reminds me of the CD player we have at the front counter at the cleaners. The laser must be dirty or scratched or something because it always skips on track four. It's this old Michael Bublé Christmas album that my manager refuses to take out of the machine, even though it's not even close to the holidays. It just skips and repeats the exact same line over and over. It drives me absolutely up the wall. I tried to clean it last week with one of those special discs, you know the ones with the tiny little brushes glued to the bottom? But I think I just made it worse. Rosa told me I should just accidentally knock the whole stereo into the trash can. Honestly, I'm tempted. Though our dumpsters out back smell so bad right now I don't even want to go near them. The seafood place next door keeps leaking their old frying oil into the alley, and the stray cats come and lick it up. It's so gross. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question again, no. I never do that. I always have to get rid of it.\n\nDoctor: Is it the fear of gaining weight that triggers your loss of control when eating?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't really know if it's the fear of gaining weight that starts it. I mean, I am terrified of gaining weight, obviously, but when I lose control, it's just... I'm not sure what triggers it exactly. It just happens. \n\nSpeaking of triggers, the bell above the door at the cleaners is driving me insane lately. Every time someone walks in, it does this really high-pitched jingle. It didn't used to bother me, but the spring is loose or something, so now it rattles for like ten seconds after the door closes. It sets my teeth on edge. Rosa says it sounds like a dog with a rusty collar scratching itself. She actually has two dogs, pugs, I think? Or maybe French bulldogs. One of them has this breathing problem where it snores even when it's awake. She showed me a video on her phone, and the screen was completely cracked. She said she dropped it in the parking lot at the grocery store. The one on Elm Street, you know? They just remodeled the produce section, but they moved all the apples to the other side, and it's so confusing. I spent twenty minutes looking for Granny Smiths last time I was there. \n\nBut yeah, I don't really know what starts the eating. I just know I can't stop once I do.\n\nDoctor: Is the fear of gaining weight what causes you to lose control when you're eating?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you literally just ask me that? Like, two seconds ago? I swear, I just told you I don't know. I really don't know what causes it. It just happens. \n\nYou're honestly worse than Mr. Henderson at the shop. He comes in every single Tuesday with these hideous plaid button-downs—like, lumberjack meets 1970s basement couch—and he always asks, \"Now, you're going to use the heavy starch on the collars, right?\" And every single week, I tell him, \"Mr. Henderson, we don't do heavy starch here, we use the light liquid kind.\" And he just nods and says, \"Good, good.\" It's like his brain just resets every seven days. His wife usually waits out in the car while he drops them off. It's this huge boat of a Buick, powder blue, with the fake wood paneling on the inside. One time she left the headlights on and the battery died right in our loading zone. The tow truck took like three hours to get there because it was pouring rain. I hate the rain, honestly. It makes all the wool coats smell like wet dog when people bring them in, and the humidity messes with the steam presses. \n\nBut seriously... I don't have a different answer for you than the one I just gave. I don't know why I lose control. I just do.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the primary reason you feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Are you... are you actually kidding me right now? You're asking me the exact same thing for the third time! I don't know why you're not hearing me, but no, I don't know if the weight thing is what starts it. What I *do* know is that once I start eating, it feels like my hands just have a mind of their own. They just keep reaching for more food, even when my brain is screaming at me to stop. It's like I'm standing outside my own body, just watching myself shove food in my mouth, and I absolutely cannot make myself quit. \n\nIt's actually a lot like watching Rosa try to fix the hem on Mrs. Gable's silk dresses. Mrs. Gable is this incredibly picky lady who lives over in the gated community by the golf course—the one with the massive stone lions out front. Anyway, Rosa always thinks she can just eyeball the hem, and I'm standing right there telling her, \"Rosa, use the measuring tape, you're going to make it crooked.\" And I just have to stand there and watch her ruin it, knowing I can't stop her because she's technically been there two years longer than me. Last time she messed up, Mrs. Gable threw an absolute fit. She had this tiny little rhinestone purse that she kept swinging around while she yelled, and one of the rhinestones actually flew off and hit the front window. I found it when I was sweeping up later. I actually kept it. I put it in the little cup holder in my car, right next to all the spare change I use for the toll booth on Route 9. They just raised the toll again, by the way. It's ridiculous. I don't even know where that money goes because the potholes on that road are big enough to swallow a tire. \n\nBut yeah, when I'm eating, I just can't stop. I'm just watching myself do it. I don't know what else to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight what makes you feel like you're losing control when you eat?\n\nPatient: Are you actually messing with me right now? That is the fourth time. You've asked me the exact same thing four times! \n\nLook, I don't know if it's the trigger for the eating, like I've been saying. But am I afraid of gaining weight? Yes. If the scale goes up even a little bit, or if my waistband feels tight under my work apron, I completely panic. It feels like I have ruined everything and I will be fat by the end of the day. \n\nAnd honestly, those work aprons do not help anything. The owner just switched to a new supplier, and they're made of this horrible stiff polyester that doesn't breathe at all. Plus, they're this ugly maroon color now. They used to be navy blue, which hid the spot-treatment chemicals way better. Now if I splash a little of the solvent on myself, it leaves this weird dark ring that looks like a grease stain. The solvent smells terrible, too. It gets in your hair and you absolutely cannot wash it out. I buy this really expensive coconut shampoo from the drugstore on the corner—the one where the automatic doors are always broken, so you have to pry them open like you're breaking in—and even that doesn't get the smell out. I was complaining to the cashier about it yesterday. Her name is Brenda, and she has these really long acrylic nails painted like little watermelons. I have no idea how she types on the register with those things. I'd be hitting three buttons at once. \n\nBut anyway, yeah. I panic about my weight all the time. I just don't know if that's what makes me lose control with the food. Can we please, please move on to a different question now?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is what drives you to eat large amounts of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Okay, seriously, are you a robot? Is this a hidden camera show? Because that is literally the fifth time you have asked me some version of that exact same question! \n\nI do not know if the fear of gaining weight is what drives it! I really don't. All I know is that when it happens, I eat so much stuff. I can go through a full bag of chips, a bunch of pastries from the corner store, whatever leftovers are sitting in the fridge, and chug a bunch of sweet drinks all in one sitting. Half the time, I don't even really notice how much is gone until it's over and I'm just looking at empty wrappers. \n\nAnd speaking of those pastries from the corner store, the guy who runs the place—Sal—he is always trying to sell me the day-old ones at full price. He thinks I don't notice, but the icing gets all crusty and flakes off everywhere when they sit out. Plus, he keeps this giant, fat orange cat in the store that just sleeps right on top of the scratch-off lottery machine. It sheds hair everywhere. I don't even buy lottery tickets anyway. My Uncle Gary used to buy them every single morning. He actually won five hundred bucks once, but then he just turned around and spent it all on more tickets and lost every single penny. He ended up living in this beat-up RV parked behind a diner for like three years after that. The diner had really good pancakes, though. They used actual real buttermilk, not that cheap powdered stuff they used to serve us in the cafeteria back in high school. I absolutely hated high school lunches. The meatloaf always bounced if you dropped it. That's probably half the reason I dropped out in tenth grade, honestly, the food was basically toxic waste. \n\nBut anyway, yes, I eat a massive amount of food really fast before I even realize it. Can we *please* talk about something else now? Because if you ask me about my fear of gaining weight one more time, I think I'm going to scream.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Are you... I can't. I literally cannot believe this. That is the exact same question again! You just flipped the words around! Are you even listening to me? I am going to lose my mind. 

Look, I've told you, I don't know what starts it! But I do try to fix it afterward. That's the part I can control. If I eat a ton of food, the next day I just won't eat anything until super late at night. Or I'll force myself to walk and exercise for hours, even when my legs are completely killing me from standing at the dry cleaners all day. 

And standing there is no joke, okay? The mats they put behind the counter are supposed to be those anti-fatigue ones, but they've been there since like 1998. They're completely flat and the edges are curling up. I actually tripped over one last Tuesday and almost knocked over a whole rack of Mr. Henderson's suits. Mr. Henderson is this lawyer who brings in like ten suits a week. I don't even know why he needs that many, he's basically retired. He always leaves those little peppermints in his pockets—the ones with the red and white stripes wrapped in clear plastic. I used to eat them sometimes when I was starving myself during the day, but one time I bit down on one and chipped my back molar. The dentist charged me three hundred dollars to fix it, and my insurance didn't cover a dime because apparently it was "cosmetic." Cosmetic! It's a back tooth! Who is looking at my back teeth? 

But yeah, I try to prevent the weight gain by starving and exercising after I mess up. That's what I do. Now please, for the love of God, ask me something else.

He's got this old cash register that makes this loud *ding* sound every time he opens it, it's almost vintage, you know? I think he said his grandfather bought it back in the seventies or something. But yeah, I'll eat the pastries, and then whatever leftovers are in the fridge, and wash it all down with a bunch of sweet drinks... all in one sitting. It's like I don't even realize how much I've eaten until it's all gone and the wrappers are just sitting there. And then the panic sets in, and I have to go throw it up.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the main reason you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... it's the main reason, definitely. I just can't stand the thought of getting bigger. It's like this voice in my head that just won't shut up about it. Just like my neighbor's dog, actually. They got this little terrier mix a few weeks ago, and it barks at absolutely everything. A leaf blows across the sidewalk? Barking. The mail carrier comes? Nonstop barking. I tried talking to them about it, they live in 4B, the couple with the weird green car... it's like a neon green, you can't miss it in the parking lot. Anyway, I asked if they could maybe keep the dog inside during the day when they're at work, but they said he has separation anxiety. Which, I get it, animals have feelings too, but it's driving me crazy when I'm trying to sleep after a long shift. But yeah, the fear of gaining weight... that's why I do it. I just have to get it out before it turns into fat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating these large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Once I start eating, it feels like my hands just keep reaching for food even while I'm telling myself to stop. It's like I'm watching myself do it from the outside and I just cannot make myself quit. It's the weirdest feeling, almost like being on autopilot. Speaking of autopilot, my car has been doing this strange thing lately where the cruise control won't turn off right away when I tap the brakes. It's a 2008 Honda, so it's getting up there in miles. I took it to my mechanic, Luis—he's got this shop down by the old textile mill—and he said it might be a sensor issue. Luis is great, though. He always has these little hard candies on his counter, the strawberry ones with the gooey centers? I love those. He said his wife buys them in bulk from some warehouse club. I should probably ask him which one, because they're really good. But yeah, with the food... I just can't stop once I start. There's no control at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time and then not thrown up afterwards?\n\nPatient: Well, sometimes... I mean, if I don't throw up, I still have to do something about it, right? So on the days after I eat way too much and don't get sick, I try to make up for it by just not eating anything at all until really late at night. Or I'll force myself to walk for miles and exercise, even when my legs are absolutely throbbing from standing at the cleaners all day. \n\nWorking the steam press is just brutal on your feet. I actually bought these memory foam inserts for my sneakers recently, the ones they advertise on TV? The commercial has this guy dropping a bowling ball on the foam, which makes zero sense when you actually think about it. Like, who is dropping bowling balls on their shoes? But I bought them anyway at the pharmacy next to the dry cleaners. The pharmacist there, Brenda, she always wears these wild earrings. Yesterday she had on these little miniature flamingos. I asked her where she gets them, and she said she makes them herself out of polymer clay in her oven. I tried getting into crafts once, knitting mostly, but I just ended up with a bunch of tangled yarn in a garbage bag. \n\nBut yeah... if I don't get rid of the food that way, I just starve myself the next day or walk until I can barely feel my feet. I just have to make up for it somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a very large amount of food in a short time and then not done anything to try to get rid of the calories?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I always have to do *something*. If I didn't, I'd just sit there feeling it all turning into fat, and I can't handle that. It's like this heavy, awful feeling. Actually, speaking of heavy, you wouldn't believe the bags of laundry some people bring in. Just yesterday, this guy brought in a duffel bag—one of those massive military-style ones—completely stuffed with wool sweaters. Who wears that many wool sweaters in this weather? And they smell like mothballs, which is just the worst smell. It gets right up into your sinuses and stays there all day. I tried drinking peppermint tea to clear it out, because my aunt used to say peppermint cures everything. She used to grow it in her backyard in these old coffee cans. Folgers, I think. The red plastic ones. She'd paint them with these little daisies. She was so artistic like that, not like me with my tangled knitting yarn. But no, to answer your question, I never just let it sit there. I always have to get rid of it or work it off somehow. I just have to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without doing anything to try to get rid of the calories afterwards?\n\nPatient: I just... didn't I just say that? No, I never do that. I always have to do something to make up for it, or I'd just go crazy thinking about it. Like I said, I have to walk or starve or... whatever. I always do something. It's like a rule in my head. Speaking of rules, the owner at the cleaners just put up this new sign about breaks. We used to be able to just take our fifteen minutes whenever things were slow, but now he wants us to clock out exactly at 10:15 and 2:15. It's so rigid. And the time clock is this ancient punch-card thing that jams half the time. My coworker, Rosa—she's the one who does the alterations—she actually got her cardigan sleeve caught in it last week. It was a nice cardigan, too, this really pretty mustard yellow. She said she got it on clearance at that department store that's closing down in the mall. I haven't been to the mall in ages. The parking is just a nightmare, especially near the food court entrance. But yeah, no, I never just eat all that and do nothing. I always have to fix it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you ate a large amount of food in a short time without doing anything afterwards to try to get rid of the calories?\n\nPatient: Are you... are you serious right now? I feel like I'm taking crazy pills. I just told you, like, three times. No. Never. I *always* have to do something. I can't just let it sit there. \n\nIt's like you're not even listening to me. My ex-boyfriend used to do that. Gary. You'd be talking right to his face and his eyes would just glaze over like he was watching a TV show behind my forehead. He was a mechanic, always had grease under his fingernails no matter how much he scrubbed. He used this orange pumice soap that smelled like fake citrus. I hate that smell. It reminds me of the bathroom cleaner they use at the bus station. I had to take the bus for a month when my car broke down—the alternator went out. It was a nightmare. The bus driver was this guy who always whistled show tunes. *Phantom of the Opera*, mostly. \n\nBut seriously, doc, how many times are you going to ask me the exact same thing? The answer is no, I never just do nothing. I always fix it. Always.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any instance where you ate a large amount of food in a short time and then didn't throw up, exercise, or restrict your food intake afterwards?\n\nPatient: I just... I honestly don't know how else to say it to you. No. There is no instance. Not one. I never, ever just eat all that and do nothing. \n\nYou're like a broken record, you know that? It actually reminds me of the CD player we have at the front counter at the cleaners. The laser must be dirty or scratched or something because it always skips on track four. It's this old Michael Bublé Christmas album that my manager refuses to take out of the machine, even though it's not even close to the holidays. It just skips and repeats the exact same line over and over. It drives me absolutely up the wall. I tried to clean it last week with one of those special discs, you know the ones with the tiny little brushes glued to the bottom? But I think I just made it worse. Rosa told me I should just accidentally knock the whole stereo into the trash can. Honestly, I'm tempted. Though our dumpsters out back smell so bad right now I don't even want to go near them. The seafood place next door keeps leaking their old frying oil into the alley, and the stray cats come and lick it up. It's so gross. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question again, no. I never do that. I always have to get rid of it.\n\nDoctor: Is it the fear of gaining weight that triggers your loss of control when eating?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't really know if it's the fear of gaining weight that starts it. I mean, I am terrified of gaining weight, obviously, but when I lose control, it's just... I'm not sure what triggers it exactly. It just happens. \n\nSpeaking of triggers, the bell above the door at the cleaners is driving me insane lately. Every time someone walks in, it does this really high-pitched jingle. It didn't used to bother me, but the spring is loose or something, so now it rattles for like ten seconds after the door closes. It sets my teeth on edge. Rosa says it sounds like a dog with a rusty collar scratching itself. She actually has two dogs, pugs, I think? Or maybe French bulldogs. One of them has this breathing problem where it snores even when it's awake. She showed me a video on her phone, and the screen was completely cracked. She said she dropped it in the parking lot at the grocery store. The one on Elm Street, you know? They just remodeled the produce section, but they moved all the apples to the other side, and it's so confusing. I spent twenty minutes looking for Granny Smiths last time I was there. \n\nBut yeah, I don't really know what starts the eating. I just know I can't stop once I do.\n\nDoctor: Is the fear of gaining weight what causes you to lose control when you're eating?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you literally just ask me that? Like, two seconds ago? I swear, I just told you I don't know. I really don't know what causes it. It just happens. \n\nYou're honestly worse than Mr. Henderson at the shop. He comes in every single Tuesday with these hideous plaid button-downs—like, lumberjack meets 1970s basement couch—and he always asks, \"Now, you're going to use the heavy starch on the collars, right?\" And every single week, I tell him, \"Mr. Henderson, we don't do heavy starch here, we use the light liquid kind.\" And he just nods and says, \"Good, good.\" It's like his brain just resets every seven days. His wife usually waits out in the car while he drops them off. It's this huge boat of a Buick, powder blue, with the fake wood paneling on the inside. One time she left the headlights on and the battery died right in our loading zone. The tow truck took like three hours to get there because it was pouring rain. I hate the rain, honestly. It makes all the wool coats smell like wet dog when people bring them in, and the humidity messes with the steam presses. \n\nBut seriously... I don't have a different answer for you than the one I just gave. I don't know why I lose control. I just do.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the primary reason you feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Are you... are you actually kidding me right now? You're asking me the exact same thing for the third time! I don't know why you're not hearing me, but no, I don't know if the weight thing is what starts it. What I *do* know is that once I start eating, it feels like my hands just have a mind of their own. They just keep reaching for more food, even when my brain is screaming at me to stop. It's like I'm standing outside my own body, just watching myself shove food in my mouth, and I absolutely cannot make myself quit. \n\nIt's actually a lot like watching Rosa try to fix the hem on Mrs. Gable's silk dresses. Mrs. Gable is this incredibly picky lady who lives over in the gated community by the golf course—the one with the massive stone lions out front. Anyway, Rosa always thinks she can just eyeball the hem, and I'm standing right there telling her, \"Rosa, use the measuring tape, you're going to make it crooked.\" And I just have to stand there and watch her ruin it, knowing I can't stop her because she's technically been there two years longer than me. Last time she messed up, Mrs. Gable threw an absolute fit. She had this tiny little rhinestone purse that she kept swinging around while she yelled, and one of the rhinestones actually flew off and hit the front window. I found it when I was sweeping up later. I actually kept it. I put it in the little cup holder in my car, right next to all the spare change I use for the toll booth on Route 9. They just raised the toll again, by the way. It's ridiculous. I don't even know where that money goes because the potholes on that road are big enough to swallow a tire. \n\nBut yeah, when I'm eating, I just can't stop. I'm just watching myself do it. I don't know what else to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight what makes you feel like you're losing control when you eat?\n\nPatient: Are you actually messing with me right now? That is the fourth time. You've asked me the exact same thing four times! \n\nLook, I don't know if it's the trigger for the eating, like I've been saying. But am I afraid of gaining weight? Yes. If the scale goes up even a little bit, or if my waistband feels tight under my work apron, I completely panic. It feels like I have ruined everything and I will be fat by the end of the day. \n\nAnd honestly, those work aprons do not help anything. The owner just switched to a new supplier, and they're made of this horrible stiff polyester that doesn't breathe at all. Plus, they're this ugly maroon color now. They used to be navy blue, which hid the spot-treatment chemicals way better. Now if I splash a little of the solvent on myself, it leaves this weird dark ring that looks like a grease stain. The solvent smells terrible, too. It gets in your hair and you absolutely cannot wash it out. I buy this really expensive coconut shampoo from the drugstore on the corner—the one where the automatic doors are always broken, so you have to pry them open like you're breaking in—and even that doesn't get the smell out. I was complaining to the cashier about it yesterday. Her name is Brenda, and she has these really long acrylic nails painted like little watermelons. I have no idea how she types on the register with those things. I'd be hitting three buttons at once. \n\nBut anyway, yeah. I panic about my weight all the time. I just don't know if that's what makes me lose control with the food. Can we please, please move on to a different question now?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is what drives you to eat large amounts of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Okay, seriously, are you a robot? Is this a hidden camera show? Because that is literally the fifth time you have asked me some version of that exact same question! \n\nI do not know if the fear of gaining weight is what drives it! I really don't. All I know is that when it happens, I eat so much stuff. I can go through a full bag of chips, a bunch of pastries from the corner store, whatever leftovers are sitting in the fridge, and chug a bunch of sweet drinks all in one sitting. Half the time, I don't even really notice how much is gone until it's over and I'm just looking at empty wrappers. \n\nAnd speaking of those pastries from the corner store, the guy who runs the place—Sal—he is always trying to sell me the day-old ones at full price. He thinks I don't notice, but the icing gets all crusty and flakes off everywhere when they sit out. Plus, he keeps this giant, fat orange cat in the store that just sleeps right on top of the scratch-off lottery machine. It sheds hair everywhere. I don't even buy lottery tickets anyway. My Uncle Gary used to buy them every single morning. He actually won five hundred bucks once, but then he just turned around and spent it all on more tickets and lost every single penny. He ended up living in this beat-up RV parked behind a diner for like three years after that. The diner had really good pancakes, though. They used actual real buttermilk, not that cheap powdered stuff they used to serve us in the cafeteria back in high school. I absolutely hated high school lunches. The meatloaf always bounced if you dropped it. That's probably half the reason I dropped out in tenth grade, honestly, the food was basically toxic waste. \n\nBut anyway, yes, I eat a massive amount of food really fast before I even realize it. Can we *please* talk about something else now? Because if you ask me about my fear of gaining weight one more time, I think I'm going to scream.\n\nDoctor: Is your eating behavior more related to feeling like you've lost control or to preventing weight gain?\n\nPatient: Are you... I can't. I literally cannot believe this. That is the exact same question again! You just flipped the words around! Are you even listening to me? I am going to lose my mind. \n\nLook, I've told you, I don't know what starts it! But I do try to fix it afterward. That's the part I can control. If I eat a ton of food, the next day I just won't eat anything until super late at night. Or I'll force myself to walk and exercise for hours, even when my legs are completely killing me from standing at the dry cleaners all day. \n\nAnd standing there is no joke, okay? The mats they put behind the counter are supposed to be those anti-fatigue ones, but they've been there since like 1998. They're completely flat and the edges are curling up. I actually tripped over one last Tuesday and almost knocked over a whole rack of Mr. Henderson's suits. Mr. Henderson is this lawyer who brings in like ten suits a week. I don't even know why he needs that many, he's basically retired. He always leaves those little peppermints in his pockets—the ones with the red and white stripes wrapped in clear plastic. I used to eat them sometimes when I was starving myself during the day, but one time I bit down on one and chipped my back molar. The dentist charged me three hundred dollars to fix it, and my insurance didn't cover a dime because apparently it was \"cosmetic.\" Cosmetic! It's a back tooth! Who is looking at my back teeth? \n\nBut yeah, I try to prevent the weight gain by starving and exercising after I mess up. That's what I do. Now please, for the love of God, ask me something else.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Are you kidding me? Are you actually kidding me right now? Did you hit a button on a tape recorder? You just asked me that! I literally just answered that! I swear, if there's a hidden camera in here, I'm going to find it and smash it. 

I told you, I don't know what starts it! But the losing control part... yeah. Once I start eating, it's like... it's like my hands just keep reaching for more food. Even while my brain is screaming at me to stop, I'm just watching myself do it and I cannot make myself quit. It's like I'm completely disconnected from my own body. 

Kind of like when you're driving and you zone out, you know? Like last week, I was driving home from the dry cleaners, and I totally zoned out and missed my exit. I ended up driving halfway to the next town over before I realized what happened. And let me tell you, the traffic on I-95 at that time of day is a complete nightmare. It's just bumper-to-bumper minivans and huge semi-trucks. I got stuck behind this one truck hauling chickens. Actual live chickens in these tiny little cages stacked up on the back. It smelled so bad, and feathers were flying everywhere, sticking to my windshield. I tried to use my wiper fluid to get them off, but the little sprayer thing on the driver's side is clogged. I asked my neighbor, Dave, to look at it for me. Dave thinks he's this master mechanic because he watches YouTube tutorials, but last time he tried to fix my brake light, he somehow blew a fuse that controls my radio. Now I have to drive in complete silence, which is awful because the local station used to play this really good 80s block on my way home. 

But anyway, yes! I feel like I lose control. I can't stop once I start. Now please, I am begging you, ask me a different question!

Are you... are you having a stroke? Is this a medical emergency? Because I am actually terrified right now. You are literally just repeating the same sentence over and over again like a broken toy! 

I... I don't know! I don't know! I just told you I don't know what starts it, but I lose control when it happens, and then I try to prevent the weight gain after! I just said all of this! What is happening right now?! 

This is exactly like what happened to my Aunt Linda's parrot. Seriously. She had this African Grey named Captain Crunch, and for like five years, he was totally normal. He'd say "Hello" and "Want a cracker" and all that normal bird stuff. But then one day, he just started screaming the word "broccoli." Over and over. Just "Broccoli! Broccoli! Broccoli!" for hours. She took him to the vet, and it turned out he had some kind of weird bird tumor pressing on his brain. They had to put him down. It was really sad, actually, because Aunt Linda loved that bird more than she loved her actual kids. Her oldest son, Mark, got arrested for stealing copper wire from construction sites, and she didn't even bail him out. She just said, "Well, actions have consequences," and went back to feeding the parrot. Mark ended up doing six months, and when he got out, he moved to Florida and started a power-washing business. I guess he's doing okay now, he posts a lot of pictures of driveways on Facebook. 

But seriously, are you okay? Do I need to call someone? Because if you ask me that question one more time, I am walking out that door and calling an ambulance for you.

I asked my manager, Dave, if we could go back to the old ones, but he said corporate signed a two-year contract, so we're stuck with them. It's like I don't even realize how much I've eaten until it's all gone and the wrappers are just sitting there. I just can't stand the thought of getting bigger. It's like this voice in my head that just won't shut up about it. Just like my neighbor's dog, actually. He always has these little hard candies on his counter, the strawberry ones with the gooey centers? I love those. He said his wife buys them in bulk from some warehouse club. I should probably ask him which one, because they're really good. But yeah, with the food... I just can't stop once I start. Actually, speaking of heavy, you wouldn't believe the bags of laundry some people bring in. Just yesterday, this guy brought in a duffel bag—one of those massive military-style ones—completely stuffed with wool sweaters. Who wears that many wool sweaters in this weather? I just know I can't stop once I do.\n\nDoctor: Is the fear of gaining weight what causes you to lose control when you're eating?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you literally just ask me that? Like, two seconds ago? I swear, I just told you I don't know. I really don't know what causes it. It just happens. \n\nYou're honestly worse than Mr. Henderson at the shop. He comes in every single Tuesday with these hideous plaid button-downs—like, lumberjack meets 1970s basement couch—and he always asks, \"Now, you're going to use the heavy starch on the collars, right?\" And every single week, I tell him, \"Mr. Henderson, we don't do heavy starch here, we use the light liquid kind.\" And he just nods and says, \"Good, good.\" It's like his brain just resets every seven days. His wife usually waits out in the car while he drops them off. It's this huge boat of a Buick, powder blue, with the fake wood paneling on the inside. It's like I'm standing outside my own body, just watching myself shove food in my mouth, and I absolutely cannot make myself quit. \n\nIt's actually a lot like watching Rosa try to fix the hem on Mrs. Gable's silk dresses. Last time she messed up, Mrs. Gable threw an absolute fit. He ended up living in this beat-up RV parked behind a diner for like three years after that. The diner had really good pancakes, though.
